(2) We put v=v(\cdot,\rho,\Lambda,p,q) in the first order variation ( 4.21 ) to get \displaystyle\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\Lambda^{*}}(c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}+c_{b}(\pi_{b}\ell_{p})(\pi_{b}v)-(\pi_{b}\ell_{q,\Lambda^{+}})(\pi_{b}v))}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0. Then we plug this into ( 4.16 ) to get the quadratic expression. The quadratic response ( 4.23 ) follows directly from ( 4.5 ) by testing with u=w-v(\cdot,\rho,\Lambda,p) . (3) This is a consequence of ( 4.6 ) and ( 4.7 ). (4) Using the exact expression of v(\cdot,\rho,\Lambda,p,q) in ( 4.20 ), we have \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|\Lambda|}\sum_{x\in\Lambda}\nabla_{x}v(\cdot,\rho,\Lambda,p,q)}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|\Lambda|}\sum_{x\in\Lambda}\nabla_{x}u(\cdot,\Lambda,q)}\right\rangle_{\rho}-\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|\Lambda|}\sum_{x\in\Lambda}\nabla_{x}v(\cdot,\rho,\Lambda,q)}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle={\overbracket{D}}_{*}^{-1}(\rho,\Lambda)q-p. Here in the second line, we apply ( 4.3 ) to the first term and ( 4.18 ) to the second term. ∎ 5. Renormalization under grand canonical ensemble Based on the diffusion matrix {\overbracket{D}}(\rho,\Lambda),{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda) defined in Proposition 4.1 and using the Einstein relation ( 1.12 ), we define the conductivity and its dual quantity on (0,1) as (5.1) \displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda):=2\chi(\rho){\overbracket{D}}(\rho,\Lambda),\qquad\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda):=2\chi(\rho){\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda). By default, when \rho\in\{0,1\} , we set \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda)=\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda)=0 . Especially, \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda) coincides with the definition ( 1.25 ). Then Corollary 4.3 also defines a limit (5.2) \displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho):=2\chi(\rho){\overbracket{D}}(\rho)=\lim_{m\to\infty}\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}). In this section, we are ready to prove the convergence rate. Proposition 5.1 . There exists an exponent \gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0 and a positive constant {C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty} such that for every L\in\mathbb{N}_{+} , (5.3) \displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1]}\left(\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\right|+\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\right|\right)\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{1}}. Although Proposition 5.1 is stated for \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L}) and \overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda_{L}) , we will still rely on {\overbracket{D}}(\rho,\Lambda) and {\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda) in the intermediate steps as they are already normalized; see (2) of Proposition 4.1 . Note that \chi(\rho) in \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L}) helps to prove the uniform convergence in \rho\in[0,1] . Throughout this section, we define the following shorthand expression (5.4) \displaystyle D_{n}:={\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}). By the subadditive quantity ( 4.6 ) and ( 4.7 ) , we know that {\overbracket{D}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) and {\overbracket{D}}_{*}^{-1}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) are decreasing. Therefore, it suffices to show the convergence rate for |{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})-{\overbracket{D}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})| , which is reduced to the decay rate of \sup_{|p|=1}J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},p,D_{m}p) after applying ( 4.19 ) with \widetilde{D}=D_{m} . In this section, we will heavily use the master quantity J(\rho,\Lambda,p,q) defined in ( 4.16 ) and its optimizer v(\cdot,\rho,\Lambda,p,q) defined in (1) of Proposition 4.7 . The notation \mathcal{Z}_{m,n}=3^{n}{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\cap{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m} is usually involved to make comparison between J in different scales. We will also use the following gap of the master quantities very often in the proof (5.5) \tau_{n}=\tau_{n}(\rho):=\sup_{p,q\in B_{1}}(J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},p,q)-J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n+1},p,q)), where B_{1}:=\{p\in{\mathbb{R}^{d}}:|p|=1\} . Our first lemma makes use of the elliptic regularity, especially the modified Caccioppoli inequality ( 2.10 ), in our master quantity. Lemma 5.2 . There exists a finite positive constant C(d,\lambda) such that, for every m\in\mathbb{N}_{+} satisfying 3^{m}>R_{0} for the constant in Proposition 2.6 , we have the following estimate (5.6) \displaystyle J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C\tau_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C3^{-m}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}|}v(\cdot,\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},p,D_{m}p)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}. Proof. We write v_{m}=v(\cdot,\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p) as a shortened version in the proof. Recall the operator {\mathsf{A}}_{L} defined in ( 2.9 ), and denote by (5.7) \displaystyle L:=3^{m}+2\mathbf{r}, as the length of localization in this proof with 3^{m}>2\mathbf{r} . Then, by ( 4.22 ), we decompose our quantity J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p) as (5.8) \begin{split}&J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant(\mathbf{I})^{\frac{1}{2}}+(\mathbf{II})^{\frac{1}{2}},\\ \mathbf{I}&:=\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m}-{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho},\\ \mathbf{II}&:=\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.\end{split} We justify at first {\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) by testing an arbitrary function f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}) \displaystyle\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{c_{b}(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})(\pi_{b}f)}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{{\mathsf{A}}_{L}\left(c_{b}(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})(\pi_{b}f)\right)}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{c_{b}(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})(\pi_{b}f)}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0. Here in the first line, since c_{b} and f are both \mathscr{F}_{\Lambda_{L}} -measurable, they commute with {\mathsf{A}}_{L} operator. In the second line, we use the fact that v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1})\subseteq\mathcal{A}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) from (1) of Proposition 4.7 . We now turn to the two terms in ( 5.8 ). As we have proved {\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) , we apply ( 4.23 ) to \mathbf{I} and get (5.9) \displaystyle\mathbf{I}=J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}). We investigate the expression of J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}) by ( 4.17 ). By Jensen’s inequality, we have \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}, and by the definition of conditional expectation, we also have \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}(c_{b}(\pi_{b}\ell_{p})(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})-(\pi_{b}\ell_{D_{m}p})(\pi_{b}{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}))}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}(c_{b}(\pi_{b}\ell_{p})(\pi_{b}v_{m+1})-(\pi_{b}\ell_{D_{m}p})(\pi_{b}v_{m+1}))}\right\rangle_{\rho}. These imply that J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;{\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1})\geqslant J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;v_{m+1}) . Putting it back to ( 5.9 ) and using ( 4.23 ) again, we have \displaystyle\mathbf{I} \displaystyle\leqslant J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;v_{m+1}) \displaystyle=\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m}-v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. We claim that the right hand side of the inequality can be controlled by C\tau_{m} defined in ( 5.5 ) with some constant only depending on the dimension d . It suffices to apply ( 4.23 ) to z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m} with z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,m} . We denote by v_{m,z}=v(\cdot,\rho,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p) to simplify the notation in the following calculation \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m}-v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,m}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(v_{m,z}-v_{m+1}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,m}}\left(J(\rho,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p;v_{m+1})\right) \displaystyle=3^{d}(J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)-J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},p,D_{m}p)). This concludes that (5.10) \displaystyle\mathbf{I}\leqslant 3^{d}\lambda\tau_{m}. Concerning \mathbf{II} , as we have verified the condition in Proposition 2.6 that {\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1}\in\mathcal{A}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) and 3^{m}>R_{0} is assumed, we can apply the modified Caccioppoli inequality ( 2.10 ) to {\mathsf{A}}_{L}v_{m+1} to get (5.11) \displaystyle\mathbf{II}\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{C3^{-2m}}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}|}v_{m+1}^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}+\theta J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},p,D_{m}p). Here the factor \theta\in(0,1) and the constant C all come from Proposition 2.6 , and only depend on d,\lambda . Combining the two estimates ( 5.10 ) and ( 5.11 ), we have J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ \leqslant\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C3^{-m}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}|}v({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\tau_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}. Noting the fact \theta\in(0,1) , we rearrange the expression above and conclude ( 5.6 ). ∎ 5.1. Flatness estimate This part gives a flatness estimate and this is the main challenge compared to its counterpart in [ 38 , Lemma 5.1] . Due to the exclusion rule, we need the weighted multiscale Poincaré inequality to show the spatial cancellation of v(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},p,D_{m}p) . The whole Section 3 is devoted to overcome the technical difficulty here. Proposition 5.3 ( L^{2} -flatness estimate) . There exists an exponent \beta(d)\in(0,\frac{1}{4}) and a constant C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty such that for every p,q\in B_{1} and m\in\mathbb{N} , (5.12) \frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}|}\left\langle{\left(v(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},p,q)-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant C3^{2m}\left(\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right). Proof. In the proof, we write v_{m+1} as a shorthand of v(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},p,q) . We apply the weighted multiscale Poincaré inequality in Proposition 3.9 , to obtain (5.13) \left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}|}(v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ \leqslant C\sum_{n=0}^{m+1}3^{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\left\langle{I_{z,n}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}, where the term I_{z,n} is the shorthand of I_{z,n}:=\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\frac{1}{2\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}, and \mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n} is the same as defined in Proposition 3.9 . Here, the factor \frac{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|} is absorbed into the constant. Recall ( 1.54 ) for \nabla_{x} . We need to treat the terms I_{z,n} . We use the comparison of Dirichlet energy with v_{n,z}=v(\rho,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},p,q) to write (5.14) \begin{split}\left\langle{I_{z,n}}\right\rangle_{\rho}&\leqslant\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ &\quad+\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-v_{m+1})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ &\quad+\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-D_{n}^{-1}q})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.\end{split} We will estimate the first term by Lemma 5.4 below, which studies the decay of variance of \nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p}) . Before applying the inequality, we first make use of some concentration of \mathbf{N}_{z,n}^{*} . We denote by \displaystyle\rho^{*}:=\left\langle{\frac{\mathbf{N}_{z,n}^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=\min\{\rho,(1-\rho)\}, as the mean of spatial average of \mathbf{N}^{*} . Case 1: high density \mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}>\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}| . For this case, the weight is of typical size and does not degenerate, so we have (5.15) \begin{split}&\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}>\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant&\frac{6}{2\chi(\rho)\rho^{*}}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant&\frac{6}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant&\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\beta n}+C\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}.\end{split} Here we observe that \rho^{*}\geqslant\chi(\rho)\geqslant\rho^{*}/2 . The last line is from Lemma 5.4 below. Note that the exponent \beta only depends on d and the constant C depends on \lambda,\mathbf{r},d as stated in Lemma 5.4 . We highlight this step, as it is where we gain the spatial cancellation from the weighted multiscale Poincaré inequality. Case 2: low density 1\leqslant\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}<\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}| . This case is quite rare by Hoeffding inequality (see [ 18 , Theorem 2.8] ) \displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}<\frac{\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|}{2}\right]\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{(\rho^{*})^{2}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|}{2}\right). We also make use of the L^{\infty} estimate from Lemma 4.4 , and obtain (5.16) \begin{split}&\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\mathbf{1}_{\{1\leqslant\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}<\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ =&\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\mathbf{1}_{\{1\leqslant\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}<\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\frac{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant&\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\mathbf{1}_{\{1\leqslant\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}<\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left|\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant&\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}<\frac{\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|}{2}\right]4(\|v_{n,z}\|_{\infty}^{2}+|D_{n}^{-1}q-p|^{2})\\ \leqslant&\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)}\exp\left(-\frac{(\rho^{*})^{2}3^{nd}}{2}\right)3^{2(d+3)n}.\end{split} Here \mathbf{N}_{z,n}^{*} can be seen as the correct normalization factor, because the number of nonzero terms in the sum is no more than \mathbf{N}_{z,n}^{*} . Then Jensen’s inequality from the second line to the third line. In the case of large scale 3^{\frac{n}{4}}\geqslant\frac{1}{\rho^{*}} , we have a natural bound \displaystyle\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)}\exp\left(-\frac{(\rho^{*})^{2}3^{nd}}{2}\right)3^{2(d+3)n}\leqslant\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)}\exp(-\frac{3^{n(d-\frac{1}{2})}}{2})3^{(2d+6)n}\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}}{\chi(\rho)}3^{-n} with the choice of constant C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}(d)=C\sup_{t\geqslant 0}e^{-(d-\frac{1}{2})}t^{2d+6}<\infty . When the scale is small 3^{\frac{n}{4}}<\frac{1}{\rho^{*}} , we do not expect much concentration, so we use a trivial bound \displaystyle\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\mathbf{1}_{\{1\leqslant\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}<\frac{1}{2}\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left|\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left|\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle C(\lambda)\leqslant\frac{C(\lambda)}{(\rho^{*})^{2}}3^{-\frac{n}{2}}\leqslant\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\frac{n}{2}}. Combining the two cases above, we end up with the following bound on the first term: \displaystyle\frac{3}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\beta n}+C\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k} The second term in ( 5.14 ) can be controlled by the gap of the subadditive quantities using ( 4.23 ) as the proof of Lemma 5.2 \displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n}}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\left.\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-v_{m+1})\right|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\left\langle{\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left\langle{\left.\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left|\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-v_{m+1})\right|^{2}\right|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m+1,n}}\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}|\nabla_{x}(v_{n,z}-v_{m+1})|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},p,q)-J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},p,q) \displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=n}^{m}\tau_{k}. Here from the first line to the second line, we use Jensen’s inequality by noting that there are no more than \mathbf{N}^{*}_{z,n} nonzero terms in the sum \sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\nabla_{x}(\cdot) ; see the similar trick in the first three lines in ( 5.16 ). From the second line to the third line, we use ( 4.23 ). Similarly, the third term in ( 5.14 ) is also naturally bounded \displaystyle\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{\mathbf{N}_{z,n}^{*}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left|\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\left\langle{\nabla_{x}(\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-D_{n}^{-1}q})|\mathcal{G}_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}}}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}|\nabla_{x}\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-D_{n}^{-1}q}|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle= \displaystyle|D_{m}^{-1}q-D_{n}^{-1}q|^{2}\leqslant C(\lambda)\sum_{k=n}^{m-1}\tau_{k}. Plugging the estimates on the three terms into ( 5.13 ), we have \left\langle{\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1}|}(v_{m+1}-\ell_{D_{m}^{-1}q-p})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{n}\left(\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\beta n}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}+\sum_{k=n}^{m}\tau_{k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}} Squaring this equation with Cauchy–Schwartz inequality, and shrinking \beta if necessary, we prove ( 5.12 ). ∎ 5.2. Variance decay of the averaged gradient In this part, we prove the variance decay of gradient. Since its proof only uses the spatial independence, all its parameters are independent of \rho . In particular, the decay exponent \beta only depends on the dimension d . Lemma 5.4 (Variance decay) . There exist \beta(d)>0 and C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty such that for every p,q\in B_{1} and n\in\mathbb{N} , we have (5.17) \frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\nabla_{x}\left(v(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},p,q)-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\beta n}+C\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}. Proof. Since \rho,p,q does not change, we write v_{n} as a shorthand for v(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},p,q) and v_{n-1,z} as a shorthand of v(\rho,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1},p,q) . Recall that \rho^{*}=\min\{\rho,1-\rho\} , which implies (5.18) \displaystyle\forall\rho\in[0,1],\qquad\frac{\rho^{*}}{2}\leqslant\chi(\rho)\leqslant\rho^{*}. From now on, we focus on the large n and make the assumption that (5.19) \displaystyle n\geqslant n_{0},\qquad n_{0}:=\min\left\{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}:3^{k}=10\mathbf{r}+\frac{1}{(\rho^{*})^{4}}\right\}. We use a comparison between scale n and scale (n-1) (5.20) \begin{split}&\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n}-\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ &\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-\ell_{D_{n-1}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ &\qquad+\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ &\qquad+\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-D_{n-1}^{-1}q}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split} We treat the three terms above separately. Concerning the third term in ( 5.20 ), we observe that there are in total no more than \mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*}(\eta) nonzero term in the sum, where recall the definition of \mathbf{N}^{*}_{0,n} in Proposition 3.9 . (5.21) \begin{split}&\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-D_{n-1}^{-1}q}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ \leqslant&\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|^{2}}\mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*}\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}}|\nabla_{x}\ell_{D_{n}^{-1}q-D_{n-1}^{-1}q}|^{2}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\rho}\\ \leqslant&\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)}\frac{2}{\rho^{*}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|^{2}}(\mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*})^{2}C|D_{n}^{-1}q-D_{n-1}^{-1}q|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ \leqslant&C|D_{n}^{-1}q-D_{n-1}^{-1}q|\leqslant C(d,\lambda)\tau_{n-1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split} Here we make use of the fact that \rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}| is sufficiently large so that \left\langle{(\mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=(\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|)^{2}+\chi(\rho)|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\leqslant 2(\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|)^{2}. Concerning the second term in ( 5.20 ), we use Jensen’s inequality to obtain \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)}\frac{2}{\rho^{*}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|^{2}}\mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\left|\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. Here, we need to use some concentration to give a control on \mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*} . We consider two different cases of \mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*} . When \mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*}\leqslant 2\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}| , we have \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)}\frac{2}{\rho^{*}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{N}_{0,n}\leqslant 2\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\left|\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)}\frac{8}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{N}_{0,n}\leqslant 2\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\left|\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)}\frac{8}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\left|\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle 8\tau_{n-1}, where the last line comes from the same argument as in Lemma 5.2 . Otherwise, when \mathbf{N}_{0,n}>2\rho|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}| , we use the L^{\infty} estimate from Lemma 4.4 to obtain \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)}\frac{2}{\rho^{*}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{N}^{*}_{0,n}>2\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|\}}\mathbf{N}_{0,n}^{*}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\left|\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)}\frac{1}{\rho^{*}}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}[\mathbf{N}^{*}_{0,n}>2\rho^{*}|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+}|]4(\|v_{n}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|v_{n-1}\|_{\infty}^{2}) \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)\rho^{*}}\exp(-2\times 3^{nd}(\rho^{*})^{2})3^{2(d+3)n} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle 3^{\frac{n}{2}}\exp(-2\times 3^{nd}3^{-\frac{n}{2}})3^{2(d+3)n} \displaystyle\leqslant \displaystyle C3^{-n}, where C=C(d)=\sup_{n\geqslant 0}\exp(-2\times 3^{dn-\frac{n}{2}})3^{(2d+8)n}<\infty . The concentration inequality is applied in the third line and the assumption ( 5.19 ) is utilized in the fourth line above. Combining the two estimates, we conclude that (5.22) \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{1}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-v_{n}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C\tau_{n-1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C3^{-\frac{n}{2}}. For the first term in ( 5.20 ), we define X_{z}:=\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}|}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1},p,q))-\ell_{D_{n-1}^{-1}q-p}\right), and expand the square \displaystyle\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\sum_{x\in z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\left(v_{n-1,z}-\ell_{D_{n-1}^{-1}q-p}\right)\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}X_{z}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}|^{2}}\sum_{z,w\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}\left\langle{X_{z}\cdot X_{w}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. Note that X_{z} is an {\mathbb{R}^{d}} -valued random vector, and here X_{z}\cdot X_{w} is the inner product between X_{z} and X_{w} . For sufficiently large n such that 3^{n}>10\mathbf{r} , there exist two cubes z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1} and w+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n-1} with distance greater than 2\mathbf{r} . Recall that X_{z}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(N_{\mathbf{r}}(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}^{+})) from (1) of Proposition 4.1 . Then we can use the local property and independence for such pair X_{z},X_{w} to get \displaystyle\left\langle{X_{z}\cdot X_{w}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=\left\langle{X_{z}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\cdot\left\langle{X_{w}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0, where the last equal sign follows from the average slope property ( 4.25 ). For other pairs, we just use Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and stationary property, which concludes (5.23) \displaystyle\left\langle{\left|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}|}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,n-1}}X_{z}\right|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\frac{3^{2d}-1}{3^{2d}}\left\langle{|X_{0}|^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. We denote left hand side of ( 5.17 ) by \sigma_{n}^{2} , then under the assumption ( 5.19 ), the estimates ( 5.21 ), ( 5.22 ) and ( 5.23 ) hold and yield \forall n\geqslant n_{0},\qquad\sigma_{n}\leqslant\theta\sigma_{n-1}+C\tau_{n-1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+C3^{-\frac{n}{2}} for some positive constant \theta:=\frac{3^{2d}-1}{3^{2d}}<1 . We implement an iteration to obtain that \displaystyle\forall n\geqslant n_{0},\qquad\sigma_{n}\leqslant\theta^{n-n_{0}}\sigma_{n_{0}}+C(d,\lambda)\sum_{k=n_{0}}^{n-1}\theta^{n-k}\tau_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}. Here we observe the fact that \theta>3^{-\frac{1}{4}} , which implies that \displaystyle\theta^{-n_{0}}\leqslant 3^{\frac{n_{0}}{4}}\leqslant 3\times\left(10\mathbf{r}+\frac{1}{(\rho^{*})^{4}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leqslant C\left(10\mathbf{r}+\frac{1}{\rho^{*}}\right). We also use the trivial bound \sigma_{0}\leqslant\lambda , which ensures that \displaystyle\forall n\geqslant n_{0},\qquad\sigma_{n}\leqslant\frac{C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})}{\chi(\rho)}\theta^{n}+C(d,\lambda)\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\theta^{n-k}\tau_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}. Squaring this and shrinking \theta , we conclude that \forall n\geqslant n_{0},\qquad\sigma_{n}^{2}\leqslant\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\beta n}+C\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\tau_{k}. It remains to prove the result when n\leqslant n_{0} . In this case, the bound is automatically true since we have a natural bound \sigma_{n}^{2}\leqslant\frac{\lambda}{\chi(\rho)}\leqslant\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)^{2}}3^{-\beta n} . This completes the proof. ∎ Remark 5.5 . Note that we can replace the bound for small n as \sigma_{n}^{2}\leqslant\frac{\lambda}{\chi(\rho)}\leqslant\frac{C}{\chi(\rho)^{1+\delta}}3^{-\beta n} if we choose sufficiently small \beta . This refined argument can be used to prove the quantitative convergence of \chi(\rho)^{1+\delta}J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p) . But since we do not need this, we keep the factor \chi(\rho)^{2} . 5.3. Iterations and track of parameters In this part, we summarize the previous steps and conclude the proof. Especially, here we need to track the dependence of the density \rho in our convergence. Recall ( 5.1 ), and combine the result ( 4.19 ) with \widetilde{D}=D_{m} defined in ( 5.4 ), (5.24) \begin{split}&\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})\right|\\ &=2\chi(\rho)\left|{\overbracket{D}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})-{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})\right|\\ &\leqslant C(d,\lambda)\chi(\rho)\left(\sup_{|p|=1}J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).\end{split} Therefore, we need to study the uniform convergence of \chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p) , where the compressibility \chi(\rho) helps control the convergence near two endpoints. We state the following lemma, which controls \chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p) by the energy gap \tau_{n} defined in ( 5.5 ) uniformly in \rho . Proof of Proposition 5.1 . We combine the estimates ( 5.6 ) and ( 5.12 ), which yields J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)\leqslant C\tau_{m}+C\left(\chi(\rho)^{-2}3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\tau_{n}\right). Then we times \chi^{2}(\rho) and obtain (5.25) \displaystyle\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,D_{m}p)\leqslant C\left(3^{-\beta m}+\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\beta(m-n)}\chi^{2}(\rho)\tau_{n}\right). The remaining part is similar to the proof of [ 11 , Proposition 2.11] and we give its sketch. We define (5.26) \displaystyle F_{m}:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},e_{i},D_{m}e_{i}), and its weighted version with \beta>0 from ( 5.25 ) (which inherits from ( 5.12 )) \displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m}:=\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\frac{\beta}{2}(m-n)}F_{n}. We also define a modified version of the gap in ( 5.5 ) (5.27) \displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{n}=\widetilde{\tau}_{n}(\rho):=\sup_{p,q\in B_{1}}\chi^{2}(\rho)(J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},p,q)-J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n+1},p,q)) Since ( 5.5 ) and ( 5.25 ) all satisfy the estimate independent of \rho , it suffices to follow the iteration in [ 11 , Proposition 2.11] . Recalling the definition J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},p,q)=\frac{1}{2}(q-D_{m}p)\cdot D_{m}^{-1}(q-{\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})p)+\frac{1}{2}p\cdot D_{m}p, then we have F_{m+1}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},e_{i},D_{m+1}e_{i})\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{d}\chi^{2}(\rho)J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},e_{i},D_{m}e_{i})\leqslant F_{m}. Therefore, we may apply ( 5.25 ) to get \displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m+1} \displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{m+1}3^{-\frac{\beta}{2}(m+1-n)}F_{n} \displaystyle\leqslant C3^{-\frac{\beta m}{2}}+\sum_{n=1}^{m+1}3^{-\frac{\beta}{2}(m+1-n)}F_{n-1} \displaystyle\leqslant C3^{-\frac{\beta m}{2}}+C\sum_{n=0}^{m}3^{-\frac{\beta}{2}(m-n)}\left(3^{-\beta n}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}3^{-\beta(n-k)}\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\right) \displaystyle\leqslant C3^{-\frac{\beta m}{2}}+C3^{-\frac{\beta m}{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\sum_{n=k}^{m}3^{\beta k-\frac{\beta}{2}n} \displaystyle\leqslant C3^{-\frac{\beta m}{2}}+C\sum_{k=0}^{m}3^{-\frac{\beta}{2}(m-k)}\widetilde{\tau}_{k}. Here all constants are finite positive and depend on \lambda,d,\mathbf{r} only. On the other hand, we have a lower bound \displaystyle F_{m}-F_{m+1}\geqslant\sum_{i=1}^{d}\chi^{2}(\rho)(J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},e_{i},D_{m}e_{i})-J(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m+1},e_{i},D_{m}e_{i}))\geqslant C\widetilde{\tau}_{m}, for some finite positive constant C=C(d) . Combining the formulas above, we conclude that there exists a constant {C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty} such that \displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m+1}\leqslant C(\widetilde{F}_{m}-\widetilde{F}_{m+1})+C3^{-\frac{\beta m}{2}}. This implies a contraction \displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m+1}\leqslant\left(\frac{C}{1+C}\right)\widetilde{F}_{m}+\left(\frac{C}{1+C}\right)3^{-\frac{\beta m}{2}}, which gives the decay of \widetilde{F}_{m} . Following ( 5.24 ) and ( 5.26 ), there exists \gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0 and C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty such that (5.28) \displaystyle\forall\rho\in[0,1],\qquad\left|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})\right|\leqslant F_{m}\leqslant\widetilde{F}_{m}\leqslant C3^{-\gamma_{1}m}. Recall the monotone convergence in Corollary 4.3 for a fixed \rho , thus as m\to\infty , the sequence \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) decreases and \overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}) increases to the same limit \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) . Then ( 5.28 ) gives the desired uniform convergence rate along the triadic cubes, and Lemma A.1 extends the result to a general cube, which concludes the proof. ∎ 6. Density-free local corrector with uniform convergence There still remains some difference between our homogenization result in Proposition 5.1 and Theorem 1.3 . The optimizer F_{L} in Theorem 1.3 is a corrector-type function in homogenization, and we have already obtained a natural candidate from previous sections (see ( 4.1 ) and (1) of Proposition 4.1 ) (6.1) \phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}:=v(\rho,\Lambda,\xi)-\ell_{\Lambda^{+},\xi}. This local corrector is of \mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-}) , but has the dependence on \rho . In this part, we explore various properties about this function at first in Sections 6.1 and 6.2 , then improve it by removing the dependence of density in Section 6.3 , and finally prove the main theorem (Theorem 1.3 ) in Section 6.4 and 6.5 . Section 6.6 is an application of the results obtained, and prepares for the hydrodynamic limit in Section 7 . 6.1. Regularity of local corrector We summarize at first some properties about our local corrector \phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi} from previous sections. Proposition 6.1 . The local corrector \phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi} satisfies the following properties. (1) (Elementary properties) \phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi} is a \mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-}) function with \left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0 , and \xi\mapsto\phi_{\rho,\Lambda,\xi} is linear. (2) (Approximation of conductivity) For every L\in\mathbb{N}_{+} , we have (6.2) \displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1],\xi\in B_{1}}\left|\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi\right|\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{1}}. Here the exponent \gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) and the constant C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) are the same as in Proposition 5.1 . (3) (Sublinearity) There exists a constant C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty , such that for every L\in\mathbb{N}_{+} , the following estimate holds for \gamma_{1}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) from Proposition 5.1 (6.3) \displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1],\xi\in B_{1}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\phi^{2}_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant CL^{2-\gamma_{1}}. Proof. (1) can be deduced from Proposition 4.1 . (2) is the consequence from Proposition 5.1 . (3) comes from Proposition 5.3 , once we put the convergence rate ( 5.3 ) to ( 5.5 ). ∎ The main task of this part is to explore the regularity on \rho . We propose the following factor to measure the one-sided bias when changing the probability: (6.4) \displaystyle\forall\rho^{\prime},\rho\in(0,1),\qquad\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}:=\max\left\{\frac{\rho^{\prime}}{\rho},\frac{1-\rho^{\prime}}{1-\rho}\right\}, This quantity is used to control the continuity with respect to a small change of the density \rho . We remark that, the role of \rho and \rho^{\prime} is not same here, since we should always view the second parameter as the targeted density and the first parameter as its perturbation. The following observation is obvious (6.5) \displaystyle|\rho^{\prime}-\rho|<\varepsilon\min\{\rho,1-\rho\}\Longrightarrow\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}\in[1,1+\varepsilon]. Especially, we only require the targeted density \rho not to be degenerated to 0 or 1 to get a good estimate, but the perturbation \rho^{\prime} can be degenerate. We also define the two-sided bias factor (6.6) \displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}:=\max\{\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho},\Theta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\}, where the role of \rho and \rho^{\prime} is symmetric. The following lemma is an example to apply the one-sided bias factor \Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho} , and it will be useful throughout the section. Lemma 6.2 . For bounded domain \Lambda\subseteq{\mathbb{Z}^{d}} and every local function f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda) , the following inequality holds for every \rho^{\prime},\rho\in(0,1) (6.7) \displaystyle\left|\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right|\leqslant\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{|\Lambda|}-1\right)\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\rho}. Proof. We make the decomposition using the canonical ensemble. Denote by X the random variable X:=\sum_{x\in\Lambda}\eta_{x} \displaystyle\left|\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right| \displaystyle=\left|\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda|}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]-\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]\right)\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,M}\right| \displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda|}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]-\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]\right|\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,M} \displaystyle=\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda|}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]}-1\right|\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,M}. Using the expression of Binomial distribution and the bound M\leqslant|\Lambda| , we obtain \displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\rho^{\prime}}\left[X=M\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[X=M\right]}-1\right|=\left|\frac{(\rho^{\prime})^{M}(1-\rho^{\prime})^{|\Lambda|-M}}{{\rho}^{M}(1-\rho)^{|\Lambda|-M}}-1\right|\leqslant\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{|\Lambda|}-1. This concludes the desired result. ∎ We now study the dependence of the density \rho in several quantities. A similar argument can be found in [ 39 , Proposition 6.1] . Proposition 6.3 (Regularity on density) . For every L\in N_{+} , every \rho,\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}\in(0,1) and \xi\in B_{1} , we have the following estimates using the factors \Theta and \widetilde{\Theta} defined respectively in ( 6.4 ) and ( 6.6 ). (1) Regularity of conductivity: we have (6.8) \displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})\leqslant\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),. and (6.9) \displaystyle|\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})|\leqslant\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}. (2) Regularity of mean: (6.10) \displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime}}\leqslant L^{2}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}) (3) Regularity of Dirichlet energy and L^{2} : if \widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}},\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\leqslant 2 , we have (6.11) L^{-2}\left\langle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\ \leqslant 10\left(\max\left\{\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}},\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\right\}-1\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}. Proof. (1) We test \phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi} in the variational problem of \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda) , and apply ( 6.7 ) to change the parameter of the grand canonical ensemble (6.12) \begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})\xi&\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ &\leqslant\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}\\ &=\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\frac{1}{2}p\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L})p.\end{split} Here because of the correlation length \mathbf{r} , the integration \sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2} is in \mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{L+2\mathbf{r}}) and we need to enlarge the power for the factor \Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho} . This proves ( 6.8 ). By exchanging the role of \rho and \rho^{\prime} , we can obtain the estimate on the other direction similarly, which concludes ( 6.9 ) using \widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho} defined in ( 6.6 ). (2) Recall that \left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=0 from (1) of Proposition 6.1 , then we apply ( 6.7 ) to \frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime}} \displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime}} \displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left|\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime}}-\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\right|^{2} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}\left\langle{|\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}|}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}\left\langle{\phi^{2}_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant L^{2}\left(\Theta_{\rho^{\prime},\rho}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}). Here we apply Jensen’s inequality from the second line to the third line, and the spectral inequality ( 2.5 ) from the third line to the fourth line. (3) To compare the Dirichlet energy, we add \phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi} as an intermediate term, which gives \frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\ \leqslant\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}. For each term, we can repeat the argument in ( 6.12 ), and conclude that (6.13) \frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\ \leqslant\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}+\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-2\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}. The L^{2} term can be done similarly, \left\langle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\ \leqslant\left\langle{\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\left\langle{\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}. Here, we hope to use Poincaré inequality, but the constant part of the function should be truncated. We take the term involving \rho,\rho^{\prime\prime} for example \left\langle{\frac{2}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\\ \leqslant\left\langle{\frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}+\frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}. The Poincaré inequality ( 2.5 ) applies to the first term, and then we can use ( 6.13 ) \displaystyle\left\langle{\frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}(\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}-\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4L^{2}}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}-\pi_{b}\phi_{\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho^{\prime\prime}} \displaystyle\leqslant 4L^{2}\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)\max\left\{|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho,\Lambda_{L}),|\overline{\mathbf{c}}|(\rho^{\prime\prime},\Lambda_{L})\right\}. Concerning the term \frac{4}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{\phi_{\rho,\Lambda_{L},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\rho^{\prime\prime}} , we apply directly ( 6.10 ). Under the assumption \widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\leqslant 2 , then we have \left(\Theta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right)^{2}\leqslant\left(\Theta_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right) and the leading order should be \left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\rho,\rho^{\prime\prime}}^{(L+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1\right) . This concludes the proof. ∎ 6.2. Convergence rate under canonical ensemble In this part, we give the convergence rate of the conductivity under the canonical ensemble. Inspired by the subadditive quantities in ( 4.1 ), we can also define their counterparts under the canonical ensemble that (6.14) \begin{split}\frac{1}{2}p\cdot\widehat{D}(\Lambda,N)p&:=\inf_{v\in\ell_{p,\Lambda^{+}}+\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-})}\left\{\frac{1}{2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)|\Lambda|}\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,N}\right\},\\ \frac{1}{2}q\cdot\widehat{D}_{*}^{-1}(\Lambda,N)q\\ &:=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{F}_{0}}\left\{\frac{1}{2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)|\Lambda|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\Lambda}^{*}}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q,\Lambda})(\pi_{b}v)-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\Lambda,N}\right\}.\end{split} Similarly to ( 5.1 ), we define the conductivity using the Einstein relation ( 1.12 ) (6.15) \displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{c}}(\Lambda,N):=2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)\widehat{D}(\Lambda,N),\qquad\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\Lambda,N):=2\chi(N/|\Lambda|)\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda,N). Here \hat{\mathbf{c}}(\Lambda,N) also coincides with the definition ( 1.28 ). Our main result in this subsection is the following proposition. Proposition 6.4 . Under Hypothesis 1.1 , there exists an exponent \gamma_{2}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0 and a positive constant C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty such that for every L,M\in\mathbb{N}_{+} , (6.16) \displaystyle\left|\hat{\mathbf{c}}(\Lambda_{L},M)-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)\right|+\left|\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)\right|\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{2}}, where \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) is the same as that defined in ( 5.2 ). We establish at first a result of the local equivalence of ensembles. Similar result can be found in [ 54 , Appendix 2] and other references. In our setting, for any \Lambda\subseteq{\mathbb{Z}^{d}} and \varepsilon\in(0,1) , we define the following set of integers such that the empirical density is not degenerate (6.17) \displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}(\Lambda):=\{M\in\mathbb{N}_{+}:\varepsilon\leqslant M/|\Lambda|\leqslant 1-\varepsilon\}. Lemma 6.5 . Let L,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{+} and M\in\mathbb{N} , we denote by \hat{\rho} the empirical density \hat{\rho}:=\frac{M}{|\Lambda_{L}|} . (1) If 10\ell^{2}\leqslant L and 0\leqslant M\leqslant L^{d} , then for any local function f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{\ell}) such that {\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N}\geqslant 0} for any N\in\mathbb{N} , we have (6.18) \displaystyle\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M}\leqslant\left(1+4\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}\right)\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}. (2) Given \varepsilon\in(0,1) , if 10\ell^{2d}\leqslant\varepsilon L^{d} and M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}(\Lambda_{L}) , then for any local function f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{\ell}) , we have (6.19) \displaystyle\left|\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}\left\langle{|f|}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}. Proof. The proof is similar to Lemma 6.2 . For f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{\ell}) , we decompose the expectation as (6.20) \displaystyle\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M}-\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}}=\sum_{M=0}^{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}-1\right)\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]\left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N}. It remains to analyze the Radon–Nikodym derivative under different setting. (1) Under this setting, because \left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N} is positive, it suffices to have an upper bound for the following probability (6.21) \displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]=\frac{{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|\choose M-N}}{{|\Lambda_{L}|\choose M}}=\frac{\left(\frac{M!}{(M-N)!}\right)\left(\frac{(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)!}{(|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|-(M-N))!}\right)}{\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|!}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|!}}. Notice that (6.22) \begin{split}&\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|!}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|!}\geqslant|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|},\qquad\frac{M!}{(M-N)!}\leqslant M^{N},\\ &\frac{(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)!}{(|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|-(M-N))!}\leqslant(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N},\end{split} thus we have an upper bound for ( 6.21 ) (6.23) \begin{split}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}&\leqslant\left(\frac{M^{N}(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}}\right)\Big/\left(\frac{M^{N}(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}}{|\Lambda_{L}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}}\right)\\ &=\left(\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}.\end{split} It suffices to give an estimate for the term \left(\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|} . Using the condition {\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\in(0,\frac{1}{10})} , we have \displaystyle\left(\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|}\leqslant\left(1+2\left(\frac{\ell}{L}\right)^{d}\right)^{\ell^{d}}\leqslant e^{\log\left(1+2\left(\frac{\ell}{L}\right)^{d}\right)\ell^{d}}\leqslant e^{2\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}}\leqslant 1+4\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}. This estimate together with ( 6.21 ), ( 6.23 ) and ( 6.18 ) gives us the desired result for positive function f . (2) Under this setting, \left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{\ell},N} can be negative, so we need two-sided estimate for the Radon–Nikodym derivative. The upper bound is already proved in ( 6.23 ), and we only need the lower bound, which is as follows (6.24) \begin{split}&\frac{|\Lambda_{L}|!}{|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|!}\leqslant|\Lambda_{L}|^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|},\qquad\frac{M!}{(M-N)!}\geqslant(M-N)^{N},\\ &\frac{(|\Lambda_{L}|-M)!}{(|\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda_{\ell}|-(M-N))!}\geqslant((|\Lambda_{L}|-M)-(|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N))^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}.\end{split} This gives us a lower bound of the proportion \displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}\geqslant\left(1-\frac{N}{M}\right)^{N}\left(1-\frac{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}{|\Lambda_{L}|-M}\right)^{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}. Because M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}(\Lambda_{L}) , we have \displaystyle 0\leqslant\max\left\{\frac{N}{M},\frac{|\Lambda_{\ell}|-N}{|\Lambda_{L}|-M}\right\}\leqslant\frac{\ell^{d}}{\varepsilon L^{d}}, which results in (6.25) \frac{\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}}\left[\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\eta_{x}=N\right]}\geqslant\left(1-\frac{\ell^{d}}{\varepsilon L^{d}}\right)^{\ell^{d}}\geqslant 1-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\ell^{2}}{L}\right)^{d}. Here we also make use of the condition 10\ell^{2d}\leqslant\varepsilon L^{d} . This estimate and ( 6.20 ) together conclude ( 6.19 ). ∎ With this local equivalence of ensembles result, we can obtain the convergence rate of diffusion matrix under the canonical ensemble. Proof of Proposition 6.4 . By similar analysis as Proposition 4.1 and Lemma 4.6 in previous sections, we obtain that (6.26) \displaystyle\mathsf{Id}\leqslant\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda,N)\leqslant\widehat{D}(\Lambda,N)\leqslant\lambda\mathsf{Id}. The strategy is the following sandwich argument : we prove that in scale 1\ll\ell\ll L , we have (6.27) {\overbracket{D}}_{*}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell})-C\ell L^{-1}\mathsf{Id}\leqslant\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)\\ \leqslant\widehat{D}(\Lambda_{L},M)\leqslant{\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell})+C\ell L^{-1}\mathsf{Id}. Then the distance from {\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell}) (resp. {\overbracket{D}}_{*}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|,\Lambda_{\ell}) ) to {\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|) bounds that from \widehat{D}(\Lambda_{L},M) (resp. \widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M) ) to {\overbracket{D}}(M/|\Lambda_{L}|) . In the following, for the convenience to implement the renormalization step, we justify the two sides in ( 6.27 ) with L=3^{m},\ell=3^{n},{m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}} , but one can easily adapt it to the general case. We always suppose that the parameters L=3^{m},\ell=3^{n} satisfy the conditions of Lemma 6.5 . We also denote by \hat{\rho}:=\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|} to lighten the notation, which can be interpreted as the empirical density of particles under the canonical ensemble. Step 1: comparison between \widehat{D}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M) and {\overbracket{D}}(\hat{\rho},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}) . We propose a sub-minimizer \ell_{\xi}+\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},\xi} for the optimization problem of \widehat{D}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M) that \displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},\xi}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\phi_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}. Here we require the \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n} in order to cutoff the influence of particles from the domain outside {\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m} . Since it is a sub-minimizer, we have (6.28) \begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\widehat{D}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\xi\\ &\leqslant\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M}\\ &\leqslant\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M}\\ &\qquad+\lambda 3^{-(m-n)}|p|^{2}.\end{split} Here the last error term \lambda 3^{-(m-n)}|p|^{2} comes from the calculation of Dirichlet energy in the boundary layer of width 3^{n} , where we do not pose any corrector and the affine function can be calculated directly. For the Dirichlet energy in the interior and when 3^{n}>\mathbf{r} , then for every z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n} , we have that \displaystyle\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}))^{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}({z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n+1}}), and z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n+1}\subseteq{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m} . Then Lemma 6.5 -(1) applies to this case ( z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},{\dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}>\mathbf{r}} ) and we have \displaystyle\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(1+3^{d(2n-m)})}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\widetilde{\phi}_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}} \displaystyle\leqslant(1+3^{d(2n-m)})\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot{\overbracket{D}}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})\xi\right). We put this back to ( 6.28 ) and concludes that (6.29) \displaystyle\widehat{D}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\leqslant{\overbracket{D}}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})+C(\lambda)(3^{-(m-n)}+3^{-d(m-2n)})\mathsf{Id}. Step 2: comparison between \widehat{D}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M) and {\overbracket{D}}_{*}(\hat{\rho},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}) . This part is quite close to that in Step 1. We make the decomposition that (6.30) \begin{split}&\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}v)-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M}\\ &=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}v)-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M}\\ &\qquad+\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}},\\ \text{other bonds}\end{subarray}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}v)-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M}\end{split} Let u_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho},q} be the maximizer for the problem \overline{\nu}_{*}(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho},q) . Then for the case z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},{\dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}>\mathbf{r}} and by the definition of \overline{\nu}_{*}(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho},q) , we also have \displaystyle\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}v)-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2\chi(\hat{\rho})|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}u_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho},q})-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}u_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho},q})^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M} \displaystyle\leqslant(1+3^{d(2n-m)})\left(\frac{1}{2}q\cdot{\overbracket{D}}_{*}^{-1}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})q\right). Here from the first line to the second line, we use the fact that u_{z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho},q} is also the maximizer under the canonical ensemble and gives positive functional; see ( 4.11 ) and Remark 4.2 for details. Then from the second line to the third line, note the positivity of the integral, Lemma 6.5 -(1) applies. We only needs to treat the boundary layer and the layer between the small cubes appearing in the third line of ( 6.30 ), which can be solved by the following uniform point-wise estimate that \displaystyle(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}v)-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\pi_{b}v)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})^{2}. Therefore, ( 6.30 ) can be reduced to \displaystyle\widehat{D}_{*}^{-1}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\leqslant{\overbracket{D}}_{*}^{-1}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})+C(\lambda)(3^{-(m-n)}+3^{-d(m-2n)})\mathsf{Id}, which implies, noting {\overbracket{D}}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})\leqslant\lambda\mathsf{Id} , that (6.31) \displaystyle\widehat{D}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\geqslant{\overbracket{D}}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})-C(\lambda)\lambda^{2}(3^{-(m-n)}+3^{-d(m-2n)})\mathsf{Id}. Step 3: conclusion. Combining ( 6.26 ), ( 6.29 ) and ( 6.31 ), we obtain {\overbracket{D}}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})-C(\lambda)(3^{-(m-n)}+3^{-d(m-2n)})\mathsf{Id}\leqslant\widehat{D}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\\ \leqslant\widehat{D}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\leqslant{\overbracket{D}}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})+C(\lambda)(3^{-(m-n)}+3^{-d(m-2n)})\mathsf{Id}. Applying ( 6.15 ) and ( 5.2 ), this is interpreted as \overline{\mathbf{c}}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})-C(\lambda)\chi(\hat{\rho})(3^{-(m-n)}+3^{-d(m-2n)})\mathsf{Id}\leqslant\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\\ \leqslant\hat{\mathbf{c}}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)\leqslant\overline{\mathbf{c}}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\hat{\rho})+C(\lambda)\chi(\hat{\rho})(3^{-(m-n)}+3^{-d(m-2n)})\mathsf{Id}. Inserting the result of homogenization ( 5.3 ), and by a choice of n that \displaystyle{d(m-2n)=\gamma_{1}n\Longleftrightarrow n=\frac{dm}{2d+\gamma_{1}}}, we obtain that \displaystyle|\hat{\mathbf{c}}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\hat{\rho})|+|\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{*}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},M)-\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\hat{\rho})|\leqslant C3^{-\left(\frac{d\gamma_{1}}{2d+\gamma_{1}}\right)m}. This is the desired result ( 6.16 ) by setting \gamma_{2}:=\frac{d\gamma_{1}}{2d+\gamma_{1}} . ∎ 6.3. Construction of density-free local corrector This subsection is devoted to removing the dependence of density in the local corrector. A first natural idea is to consider the following variational problem for bounded set \Lambda\subseteq{\mathbb{Z}^{d}} and \xi\in{\mathbb{R}^{d}} (6.32) \displaystyle\mu(\Lambda,\xi):=\inf_{v\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-})}\sup_{\rho\in[0,1]}\left\{\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+v))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi\right\}. As expected, the uniform convergence can be improved as follows. Proposition 6.6 . There exists an exponent \gamma_{3}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0 and a constant {C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty} such that for every L\in\mathbb{N}_{+} and \xi\in B_{1} , we have (6.33) \displaystyle 0\leqslant\mu(\Lambda_{L},\xi)\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{3}}. Compared with ( 4.1 ), the variational problem ( 6.32 ) is more complicated. Therefore, instead of attacking ( 6.32 ) directly, we turn to construct one sub-minimizer with a good uniform convergence for \rho\in[0,1] . This is the main task in the remaining part of this subsection. We are inspired by the function used to prove the qualitative version of ( 1.22 ) in the previous work [ 36 , Lemma 2.1] , where the main idea is to make the linear combination of the local corrector using the empirical density. Thus, we propose our density-free local corrector built on ( 6.1 ) (6.34) \displaystyle\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\phi_{\overline{\eta}_{0}\vee\varepsilon\wedge(1-\varepsilon),z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}. Recall \mathcal{Z}_{m,n}=3^{n}{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\cap{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m} . Here \overline{\eta}_{0} is a (random) empirical density defined as {\overline{\eta}_{0}:=\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}} . The mapping \rho\mapsto\rho\vee\varepsilon\wedge(1-\varepsilon) restricts the density away from 0 and 1 . That is, we make the truncation both for the spatial boundary layer and for the density, in order to avoid the perturbation from the rare but degenerate cases. More explicitly, recall the notation \mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}(\Lambda) defined in ( 6.17 ), and denote by M_{*},M^{*} (6.35) \displaystyle M_{*}:=\min\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}),\qquad M^{*}:=\max\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}), then we can rewrite this corrector as (6.36) \hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}=\sum_{M=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\phi_{\frac{M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right\}}. When n,\varepsilon is well-chosen with respect to m , this corrector will give us uniform convergence under all grand canonical ensembles. Proposition 6.7 . There exist an exponent \gamma_{4}(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0 and a constant {C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty} such that for every m\in\mathbb{N}_{+} , by setting n:=\lfloor\frac{m}{9d+3}\rfloor and \varepsilon:=3^{-\frac{2dm}{9d+3}} , we have (6.37) \displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1],\xi\in B_{1}}\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi\right|\leqslant C3^{-\gamma_{4}m}. Proof. We assume \mathbf{r}\ll 3^{n}\ll 3^{m} and 0<\varepsilon\ll 1 with n,\varepsilon to be determined in the end. Since \hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}) , we compare it with the variational problem ( 4.1 ), which gives (6.38) \displaystyle\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})\xi\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi. Thus it suffices to give the upper bound, and we can decompose the sum as (6.39) \begin{split}&\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}+\mathbf{III},\\ \mathbf{I}&:=\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}},\dist(b,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho},\\ \mathbf{II}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}\setminus({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-})^{*}\\ \dist(b,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})\leqslant 3^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho},\\ \mathbf{III}&:=\sum_{b\in({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-})^{*}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.\end{split} Roughly, \mathbf{I} is the main contribution, while the terms \mathbf{II} and \mathbf{III} come from the boundary layer. Since the order of boundary layer is 3^{(d-1)m} , they vanish after the normalization \frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|} when m\nearrow\infty . See Figure 3 for an illustration. We treat these three terms one by one in the remaining paragraphs. Figure 3. An illustration for the decomposition in ( 6.39 ): the bonds in the term \mathbf{I} , \mathbf{II} and \mathbf{III} are respectively marked in blue, green and red. Step 1: term \mathbf{I} . This is the case that the bonds stay in the interior, and is the main contribution of the Dirichlet energy. This term can be further decomposed as \displaystyle\mathbf{I}\leqslant\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. We focus on one term (z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}) above and b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}} , and have the following observation (6.40) \pi_{b}\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}(\eta)=\sum_{M=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right\}}\pi_{b}\phi_{\frac{M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}(\eta), because the Kawasaki operator \pi_{b} is conservative for the number of particles in {\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-} and only one local corrector is perturbed. Therefore, we apply the decomposition of canonical ensemble for the sum over \overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}} \displaystyle\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\sum_{M=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{M,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}} \displaystyle\leqslant(1+4\cdot 3^{d(2n-m)})\sum_{M=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right] \displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}. We use the definition ( 6.1 ) that \ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}=v\left(M/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right) from the first line to the second line. From the second line to the third line, we use the local equivalence of ensembles in ( 6.18 ) and need to assume (6.41) \displaystyle 1\leqslant n<\frac{m}{2}. We then study the last line and distinguish it in 3 cases Step 1.1: case M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}) .
