Then there is no bias between the probability space and the associated corrector, thus we have (6.42) \forall M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}),\quad\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}\\ =\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(M/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})\xi\right). Step 1.2: case 0\leqslant M\leqslant M_{*} . For this case, we have the following estimate \displaystyle\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}} \displaystyle=\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M_{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}} \displaystyle\leqslant\Theta_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M_{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}. Here from the second line to the third line, we use the regularity estimate ( 6.7 ), which gives an one-sided bias factor \Theta_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}} . This factor will not explode because the targeted density \frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|} does not degenerate; recall the remark around ( 6.5 ). More precisely, using the definition of ( 6.4 ), M\leqslant M_{*} and \frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}\simeq\varepsilon\ll 1 , we have \displaystyle\Theta_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}=\max\left\{\frac{M}{M_{*}},\frac{1-\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}{1-\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}\right\}=\frac{1-\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}{1-\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}\leqslant 1+\frac{\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}{1-\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}\leqslant 1+2\varepsilon, which results in \displaystyle\Theta_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\frac{M_{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\leqslant(1+2\varepsilon)^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\leqslant 1+10\cdot 3^{dn}\cdot\varepsilon. Here we also need to assume (6.43) \displaystyle 0<\varepsilon\ll 3^{-dn}. Using the stationarity, this concludes that (6.44) \forall 0\leqslant M<M_{*},\quad\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}\\ \leqslant\left(1+10\cdot 3^{dn}\cdot\varepsilon\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(M_{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})\xi\right). Step 1.3: case M_{*}<M<|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}| . This case is similar to Step 1.2, and we have (6.45) \forall M_{*}<M<|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,\quad\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{(z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}v\left(M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}}\\ \leqslant\left(1+10\cdot 3^{dn}\cdot\varepsilon\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(M^{*}/|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n})\xi\right). Combing ( 6.42 ), ( 6.44 ), ( 6.45 ) and the spatial homogeneity from (2) of Hypothesis 1.1 , we obtain an estimate of the term \mathbf{I} under the assumption ( 6.41 ) and ( 6.43 ) (6.46) \mathbf{I}\leqslant\left(1+10\cdot 3^{d(2n-m)}+10\cdot 3^{dn}\cdot\varepsilon\right)\sum_{M=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\\ \left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\frac{M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right). We aim to compare this quantity with the target \frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi , which relies on the concentration of measure and the regularity of \overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right) . We summarize this as Step 1.4. Step 1.4: regularity on density. Recall the Markov inequality of density (6.47) \displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\left|\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}-\rho\right|>\delta\right]\leqslant\frac{\mathbb{V}\!\mathrm{ar}_{\rho}[\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}]}{\delta^{2}}=\frac{\rho(1-\rho)}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|\delta^{2}}. We choose \delta>0 in function of \rho and distinguish two cases. Viewing the assumption ( 6.43 ), here the threshold is tighter than \varepsilon . Step 1.4.1: case \rho\in[3^{-dn},1-3^{-dn}] . We choose the window \delta:=3^{-3dn} for such case, then treat the regime \left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta at first in ( 6.46 ) \displaystyle\sum_{M\in\mathbb{N},\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\frac{M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right) \displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{M\in\mathbb{N},\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)\Theta_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\rho}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}} \displaystyle\leqslant(1+3^{dn}\delta)^{2\cdot 3^{dn}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right) \displaystyle\leqslant(1+4\cdot 3^{-dn})\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right). Here because of the assumption ( 6.43 ), we have {M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}=M} for all \left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta . Then we apply ( 6.8 ) from the first line to the second line. From the second line to the third line, we apply ( 6.5 ) to give an upper bound for \Theta_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\rho}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}} \displaystyle\forall\rho\in[3^{-dn},1-3^{-dn}],\quad\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta \displaystyle\Longrightarrow\frac{\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|}{\min\{\rho,1-\rho\}}<3^{dn}\delta \displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq.RMKTheta}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}\Theta_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\rho}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}\leqslant(1+3^{dn}\delta)^{2\cdot 3^{dn}}. Finally, we insert the choice \delta=3^{-3dn} from the third line to the fourth line. For the regime \left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\geqslant\delta , we just use the Markov inequality ( 6.47 ) and the trivial bound of \overline{\mathbf{c}} \displaystyle\sum_{M\in\mathbb{N},\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\geqslant\delta}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\frac{M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)\leqslant\lambda 3^{d(6n-m)}. Combing the two estimates above and ( 6.46 ), we conclude the estimate for \mathbf{I} under the condition \rho\in[3^{-dn},1-3^{-dn}] (6.48) \displaystyle\mathbf{I}\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)+C(\lambda)\left(3^{dn}\varepsilon+3^{-dn}+3^{d(6n-m)}\right). Step 1.4.2: case \rho\in[0,3^{-dn})\cup(1-3^{-dn},1] . We choose the window \delta:=3^{-\frac{dm}{2}} , then for {\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta} , we have \frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}\in[0,2\cdot 3^{-dn}]\cup[1-2\cdot 3^{-dn},1] since we assume ( 6.41 ). Then with the a priori bound \left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)\leqslant\chi(\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}) , we have \displaystyle\sum_{M\in\mathbb{N},\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\frac{M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right) \displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{M\in\mathbb{N},\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\leqslant\delta}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\chi\left(\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}\right) \displaystyle\leqslant C(\lambda)3^{-dn}. For {\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\geqslant\delta} , we use Markov inequality ( 6.47 ) to give \displaystyle\sum_{M\in\mathbb{N},\left|\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|}-\rho\right|\geqslant\delta}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\frac{M\vee M_{*}\wedge M^{*}}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)\leqslant C(\lambda)3^{-dn}. Therefore, under ( 6.41 ) and ( 6.43 ), the estimate in ( 6.46 ) has an upper bound for \rho\in[0,3^{-dn})\cup(1-3^{-dn},1] (6.49) \displaystyle\mathbf{I}\leqslant C(\lambda)3^{-dn}. Combing ( 6.48 ) and ( 6.49 ), we conclude the uniform estimate for Step 1 that (6.50) \displaystyle\mathbf{I}\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)+C(\lambda)\left(3^{dn}\varepsilon+3^{-dn}+3^{d(6n-m)}\right). Step 2: term \mathbf{II} . This term is the contribution of the boundary layer. For this case, because the local correctors and the indicator are both invariant when applying \pi_{b} , we have \pi_{b}\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}(\eta)=0 . The only contribution comes from the affine function, which gives us (6.51) \displaystyle\mathbf{II}\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}\setminus({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-})^{*}\\ \dist(b,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})\leqslant 3^{n}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}\ell_{\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\lambda\chi(\rho)3^{(n-m)}. This term is small under the assumption ( 6.41 ). Step 3: term \mathbf{III} . This term is also the contribution of the boundary layer, but its estimate is more delicate. We denote by b=\{y_{1},y_{2}\} , where y_{1}\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-} and y_{2}\in\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m} . The operator \pi_{y_{1},y_{2}} makes difference only when \eta_{y_{1}}\neq\eta_{y_{2}} . For example, for the case \eta_{y_{1}}=1 , \eta_{y_{2}}=0 , we have \displaystyle(\pi_{y_{1},y_{2}}\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi})(\eta)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\eta_{y_{1}}=1,\eta_{y_{2}}=0\right\}} \displaystyle=\left(\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}(\eta^{y_{1},y_{2}})-\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}(\eta)\right)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\eta_{y_{1}}=1,\eta_{y_{2}}=0\right\}} \displaystyle=\sum_{M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-})}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\left(\phi_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}-\phi_{\frac{M+1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}\right)\right)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-},x\neq y_{1}}\eta_{x}=M\right\}}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\eta_{y_{1}}=1,\eta_{y_{2}}=0\right\}}. Here we have \phi_{\rho,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}(\eta^{y_{1},y_{2}})=\phi_{\rho,z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}(\eta) , because y_{1},y_{2} are both far from the support of the local function when 3^{n}\geqslant\mathbf{r} . Meanwhile, the operator \pi_{y_{1},y_{2}} will change the empirical density, that is the perturbation in the third line. We should also remark that such perturbation will vanish when M\notin\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}) , since there is a regularization of density \rho\mapsto\rho\vee\varepsilon\wedge(1-\varepsilon) in our definition ( 6.34 ). The case \eta_{y_{1}}=0,\eta_{y_{2}}=1 is similar. As the indicator \mathbf{1}_{\left\{\eta_{y_{1}}=1,\eta_{y_{2}}=0\right\}} is independent of the other terms in the last line under \mathbb{P}_{\rho} , we obtain (6.52) \left\langle{c_{y_{1},y_{2}}(\pi_{y_{1},y_{2}}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ \leqslant 2\lambda\chi(\rho)\left(1+\sum_{M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-})}\mathbb{P}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{x\in{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-},x\neq y_{1}}\eta_{x}=M\right]\left\langle{\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}\right), where we define \Delta_{M,z} to simplify the notation (6.53) \displaystyle\Delta_{M,z}:=\phi_{\frac{M+1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}-\phi_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi},\qquad M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}). Because there are 3^{(d-1)m} terms like ( 6.52 ) from boundary layer b\in({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-})^{*} and the normalization factor is \frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|} , our object is to show that each term above is of order O(3^{Kn+sm}) with some s\in(0,1) and K\in\mathbb{R}_{+} . Then roughly we get an estimate (6.54) \displaystyle\mathbf{III}\leqslant\sum_{b\in({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-})^{*}}\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant O(3^{Kn+(s-1)m}). By a careful choice of n\ll m , we can make the contribution from \mathbf{III} small. To obtain the estimate above, we also need to treat the spatial cancellation in \left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2} . Thus, we develop it as \left\langle{\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}\\ +\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},z\neq z^{\prime}\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n},\dist(z^{\prime},\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}. We treat the diagonal terms and off-diagonal terms separately. Step 3.1: the diagonal term \left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M} . For this term, we use the local equivalence of ensembles from ( 6.18 ) (6.55) \displaystyle\left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M} \displaystyle\leqslant(1+4\cdot 3^{d(2n-m)})\left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}. Assuming that n<\frac{m}{2} as ( 6.41 ), the factor (1+4\cdot 3^{d(2n-m)}) is smaller than 2 . To simplify the notation, we introduce the following shorthand expression (6.56) \displaystyle\hat{\rho}:=\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\qquad\hat{\rho}^{\prime}:=\frac{M+1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|},\qquad\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}:=\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|-1}. Then for M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}) , as the difference between \hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime} is roughly 3^{-dm} and the three terms are not degenerate at 0 or 1 , we have an estimate for the two-sided bias factor of ( 6.6 ) (6.57) \displaystyle\forall M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}),\qquad 1\leqslant\widetilde{\Theta}_{\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}},\widetilde{\Theta}_{\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}\leqslant 1+3^{-dm}\varepsilon^{-1}. Insert the notation ( 6.56 ) to ( 6.53 ), the quantity \Delta_{M,z} has a clear expression (6.58) \displaystyle\Delta_{M,z}=\phi_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}-\phi_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}, and \left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|-1}} is just the expectation under the grand canonical ensemble of density \hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime} for difference between the correctors of densities \hat{\rho} and \hat{\rho}^{\prime} (6.59) \displaystyle\left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}|-1}}=\left\langle{(\phi_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}-\phi_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}. Therefore, we use the continuity in density ( 6.11 ) and ( 6.57 ) (6.60) \begin{split}\left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}&\leqslant 2\left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}\\ &\leqslant 10\lambda\cdot 3^{(d+2)n}\left(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}+\widetilde{\Theta}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-2\right)\\ &\leqslant 20\lambda\cdot 3^{(d+2)n}\cdot\left(\left(1+3^{-dm}\varepsilon^{-1}\right)^{3^{dn}}-1\right)\\ &\leqslant 20\lambda\cdot 3^{(2d+2)n}\cdot 3^{-dm}\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}.\end{split} Here we also need to add an assumption (6.61) \displaystyle 3^{dn}\ll 3^{dm}\cdot\varepsilon. Under this assumption, the contribution of the diagonal terms are (6.62) \displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\left(\Delta_{M,z}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M} \displaystyle\leqslant 20\lambda\cdot 3^{d(m-n)}\cdot 3^{(2d+2)n}\cdot 3^{-dm}\cdot\varepsilon^{-1} \displaystyle=20\lambda\cdot 3^{(d+2)n}\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}. Step 3.2: the off-diagonal term \left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M} . Before starting the proof, we remark that the error in each off-diagonal term should be smaller than that of the diagonal term. A naive application of Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and ( 6.60 ) does not work here, because the factor 3^{-dm} in ( 6.60 ) is not enough to dominate the total number of 3^{2d(m-n)} off-diagonal terms. We observe that \left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M} is actually asymptotically independent, and we need a decoupling inequality to justify it. Thus we make the following decomposition (6.63) \begin{split}&\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}\\ &=\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}+\left(\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}-\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}\right)\\ &=\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}+\left(\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}-\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}\right).\end{split} From the second line to the third line, we use the fact that under \mathbb{P}_{\rho} , the variables \Delta_{M,z} and \Delta_{M,z^{\prime}} are independent and of the same law. We continue to estimate the two terms respectively. For the first term in ( 6.63 ), we make use of the definition ( 6.58 ). It transforms the estimate as the regularity of mean \displaystyle\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}=\left\langle{\phi_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}-\phi_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}, so ( 6.10 ) applies (6.64) \begin{split}\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}&\leqslant 2\left\langle{\phi_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}+2\left\langle{\phi_{\hat{\rho},z+{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n},\xi}}\right\rangle^{2}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}\\ &\leqslant 2\lambda\cdot 3^{(d+2)n}\left((\widetilde{\Theta}_{\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1)^{2}+(\widetilde{\Theta}_{\hat{\rho}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1)^{2}\right)\\ &\leqslant 4\lambda\cdot 3^{(d+2)n}\cdot\left(\left(1+3^{-dm}\varepsilon^{-1}\right)^{3^{dn}}-1\right)^{2}\\ &\leqslant 4\lambda\cdot 3^{(3d+2)n}\cdot 3^{-2dm}\cdot\varepsilon^{-2}.\end{split} Here from the second line to the third line, we use the estimate of the two-sided bias factor in ( 6.57 ) for M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}) . We also need to assume ( 6.61 ) from the third line to the fourth line. Compare the regularity of mean ( 6.10 ) and of L^{2} ( 6.11 ), we gain another factor of type (\widetilde{\Theta}_{\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}}^{(3^{n}+2\mathbf{r})^{d}}-1) , that is why we have 3^{-2dm} in the last line. For the second term in ( 6.63 ), we use the local equivalence of ensembles. Especially, since \Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}} is not necessarily positive, we need apply the version of ( 6.19 ), which requires some supplementary conditions. These conditions are satisfied as we recall that M\in\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{-}) and assume ( 6.61 ). Then we obtain (6.65) \begin{split}&\left|\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}-\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}\right|\\ &\leqslant 3^{d(2n-m)}\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}\cdot\left\langle{|\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}|}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}\\ &\leqslant 3^{d(2n-m)}\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}\cdot\left\langle{(\Delta_{M,z})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\frac{M}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}|-1}}\\ &\leqslant 20\lambda\cdot 3^{(4d+2)n}\cdot 3^{-2dm}\cdot\varepsilon^{-2}.\end{split} Here we use Cauchy–Schwarz inequality from the second line to the third line, and insert the estimate ( 6.60 ) in the last line. Finally, we also gain the factor 3^{-2dm} . We combine the estimates ( 6.64 ) and ( 6.65 ) and obtain an upper bound for the off-diagonal terms (6.66) \begin{split}&\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{m,n},z\neq z^{\prime}\\ \dist(z,\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n},\dist(z^{\prime},\partial{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m})>3^{n}\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\Delta_{M,z}\Delta_{M,z^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}^{-}_{m}\setminus\{y_{1}\},M}\right|\\ &\leqslant 20\lambda\cdot 3^{2d(m-n)}\cdot 3^{(4d+2)n}\cdot 3^{-2dm}\cdot\varepsilon^{-2}\\ &=20\lambda\cdot 3^{(2d+2)n}\cdot\varepsilon^{-2}.\end{split} The error ( 6.66 ) from the off-diagonal terms is the leading order compared to ( 6.62 ) from the diagonal terms. We put them back to ( 6.54 ) and ( 6.52 ), and obtain the estimates for the contribution from \mathbf{III} (6.67) \displaystyle\mathbf{III}\leqslant 20\lambda\cdot 3^{-m}\cdot\left(3^{(2d+2)n}\cdot\varepsilon^{-2}+3^{(d+2)n}\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}\right). Step 4: track of parameters. We collect all the estimates from ( 6.50 ), ( 6.51 ) and ( 6.67 ), and put them back to ( 6.39 ) to obtain \frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)\\ \qquad+C(\lambda)\left(3^{dn}\varepsilon+3^{-dn}+3^{d(6n-m)}+3^{(n-m)}+3^{(2d+2)n-m}\varepsilon^{-2}\right)\mathsf{Id}. The choice of parameters should satisfy the assumptions ( 6.41 ), ( 6.43 ), ( 6.61 ), and also make the upper bound above be small. A possible choice is \displaystyle\varepsilon=3^{-2dn},\qquad n=\left\lfloor\frac{m}{9d+3}\right\rfloor, which gives \displaystyle\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho,{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{n}\right)\xi\right)+C(\lambda)3^{-\frac{m}{12}}. Then we apply the uniform estimate from Proposition 5.3 , which gives us \displaystyle\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}\left(\rho\right)\xi+C(d,\lambda)3^{-\frac{(1\wedge\gamma_{1})m}{12}}. Together with ( 6.38 ), this concludes ( 6.37 ) by setting \gamma_{4}:=\frac{(1\wedge\gamma_{1})}{12} . ∎ Proof of Proposition 6.6 . Viewing Proposition 4.1 and Corollary 4.3 , for any {v\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda^{-})} , we have \displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda^{*}}}\left\langle{\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+v))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda)\xi\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi, thus \mu(\Lambda,\xi)\geqslant 0 . Moreover, Proposition 6.7 implies \displaystyle 0\leqslant\mu({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},\xi)\leqslant\frac{1}{|{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}|}\left\langle{\sum_{b\in\overline{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}^{*}}}\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}(\ell_{\xi}+\hat{\phi}^{(\varepsilon)}_{m,n,\xi}))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi\leqslant C3^{-\gamma_{4}m}, then we prove ( 6.33 ) along a subsequence \{{\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}} . Like (4) of Proposition 4.1 , we can prove that \mu({\lx@scalerel@obj{\square}}_{m},\xi) is a subadditive quantity, so Lemma A.1 applies and generalizes the uniform convergence ( 6.33 ) to general cubes \Lambda_{L} with \gamma_{3}:=\gamma_{4} . ∎ 6.4. Stationary corrector Recall that we have two definitions of the conductivity, that is ( 1.15 ) for \mathbf{c}(\rho) and ( 5.2 ) for \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) . In this part, we will establish the identity \mathbf{c}(\rho)=\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) , so the results proved in the previous sections are indeed for the convergence to \mathbf{c}(\rho) . Secondly, we will give the concrete construction of \Phi_{L} valid for ( 1.22 ). We first establish several auxiliary lemmas on the stationary function. Recall that a mapping y\mapsto f(y,\eta) is stationary iff f(y,\eta)=\tau_{y}f(0,\eta)=f(0,\tau_{y}\eta) . Lemma 6.8 . The following properties hold. (1) For every x,y,z\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}} , we have (6.68) \displaystyle\pi_{x+z,y+z}\tau_{z}=\tau_{z}\pi_{x,y}. (2) For every integer 1\leqslant i\leqslant d , every \xi\in{\mathbb{R}^{d}} and every local function f\in\mathcal{F}_{0} , the following mapping is stationary. (6.69) \displaystyle y\mapsto c_{y,y+e_{i}}\pi_{y,y+e_{i}}\left(\ell_{\xi}+\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\right)^{2}. (3) For every \xi\in{\mathbb{R}^{d}},f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{L}^{-}) , we have (6.70) \displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\big(\ell_{\xi}+\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\big)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\left\langle{c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}\left(\ell_{\xi}+f\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. Proof. The identity ( 6.68 ) can be proved by a direct verification. To prove ( 6.69 ), it suffices to prove (6.71) \displaystyle c_{y,y+e_{i}}\pi_{y,y+e_{i}}\left(\ell_{\xi}+\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\right)^{2}=\tau_{y}\left(c_{0,0+e_{i}}\pi_{0,e_{i}}\left(\ell_{\xi}+\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\right)^{2}\right). We start from its right-hand side and evaluate the term one by one. From the definition of c_{y,y+e_{i}} , we have {\tau_{y}c_{0,0+e_{i}}=c_{y,y+e_{i}}} . For the term \ell_{\xi} , we have \displaystyle\tau_{y}\pi_{0,e_{i}}\ell_{\xi} \displaystyle=\tau_{y}\left(-\xi\cdot e_{i}(\eta_{e_{i}}-\eta_{0})\right)=-\xi\cdot e_{i}(\eta_{y+e_{i}}-\eta_{y})=\pi_{y,y+e_{i}}\ell_{\xi}. For the term involving f , we apply ( 6.68 ) \displaystyle\tau_{y}\pi_{0,e_{i}}\left(\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\right) \displaystyle=\pi_{y,y+e_{i}}\tau_{y}\left(\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\right) \displaystyle=\pi_{y,y+e_{i}}\left(\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x+y}f\right) \displaystyle=\pi_{y,y+e_{i}}\left(\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\right). Then we obtain ( 6.71 ) and establish the stationary property. Finally, we verify the inequality ( 6.70 ). Notice that \pi_{y,y+z}\tau_{x}\ell_{\xi}=\pi_{y,y+z}\ell_{\xi} , we have (6.72) \begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\big(\ell_{\xi}+\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{x}f\big)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ &=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\big(\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L}}\tau_{x}(\ell_{\xi}+f)\big)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}\\ &\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L}}\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\left(\tau_{x}(\ell_{\xi}+f)\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}.\end{split} From the first line to the second line, we also use the fact that f\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{L}^{-}) , so the derivative vanish when translation is outside \Lambda_{L} . From the second line to the third line above, we apply Jensen’s inequality. Then we simplify the result \displaystyle\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\left(\tau_{x}(\ell_{\xi}+f)\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\left\langle{\tau_{-x}c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\left(\tau_{x}(\ell_{\xi}+f)\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle=\left\langle{c_{-x,-x+e_{i}}\left(\pi_{-x,-x+e_{i}}\left(\ell_{\xi}+f\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. The equality in the first line comes from the stationarity of \mathbb{P}_{\rho} , i.e. \left\langle{\tau_{x}F}\right\rangle_{\rho}=\left\langle{F}\right\rangle_{\rho} for all x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}} , while the equality from the second line comes from ( 6.68 ). We put this identity back to ( 6.72 ) and conclude the desired result \displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\big(\ell_{\xi}+\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L}}\tau_{x}f\big)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L}}\left\langle{c_{-x,-x+e_{i}}\left(\pi_{-x,-x+e_{i}}\left(\ell_{\xi}+f\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\left\langle{c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}\left(\ell_{\xi}+f\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. ∎ With the preparation of Lemma 6.8 and Proposition 6.6 , we can now prove the main result in this subsection. Proposition 6.9 . For every \rho\in[0,1] , the quantities defined in ( 1.15 ) and ( 5.2 ) coincide \displaystyle\mathbf{c}(\rho)=\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho). Moreover, let \phi_{\Lambda,\xi} be an optimizer for \mu(\Lambda,\xi) defined in ( 6.32 ) and \Phi_{L}\in\mathcal{F}^{d}_{0}(\Lambda_{L}) be defined as (6.73) \displaystyle\Phi_{L}:=\left(\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\phi_{\Lambda_{L},e_{1}},\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\phi_{\Lambda_{L},e_{2}},\cdots,\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\phi_{\Lambda_{L},e_{d}}\right), then there exists an exponent \gamma(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0 and a positive constant C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty , such that (6.74) \displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in[0,1]}|\mathbf{c}(\rho;\Phi_{L})-\mathbf{c}(\rho)|\leqslant CL^{-\gamma}. Proof. The proof is similar to the sandwich argument in ( 6.27 ) and can be divided the into three steps. Similar arguments can be found in [ 38 , Theorem B.1] . Step 1: \mathbf{c}(\rho)\leqslant\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) . We recall ( 1.14 ) that \displaystyle\xi\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho;\Phi_{L})\xi=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\xi\cdot\left\{e_{i}(\eta_{e_{i}}-\eta_{0})-\pi_{0,e_{i}}(\sum_{y\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{y}\Phi_{L})\right\}\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. and notice the fact that \displaystyle\xi\cdot x(\eta_{x}-\eta_{0}) \displaystyle=-\pi_{0,x}\ell_{\xi}, \displaystyle\xi\cdot\Phi_{L} \displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\phi_{\Lambda_{L},\xi}, where the second identity comes from the linear map \xi\mapsto\phi_{\Lambda_{L},\xi} . Therefore, we have \displaystyle\xi\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho;\Phi_{L})\xi \displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\langle{c_{0,e_{i}}\left(\pi_{0,e_{i}}\left(\ell_{\xi}+\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{y\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}\tau_{y}\phi_{\Lambda_{L},\xi}\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{b\in\overline{\Lambda_{L}^{*}}}\left\langle{c_{b}\left(\pi_{b}\left(\ell_{\xi}+\phi_{\Lambda_{L},\xi}\right)\right)^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho} \displaystyle\leqslant\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi+\mu(\Lambda_{L},\xi), where we apply ( 6.70 ) from the first line to the second line and ( 6.32 ) from the second line to the third line. Using the estimate ( 6.33 ) about \mu(\Lambda_{L},\xi) , we obtain an important inequality chain with C,\gamma_{3} independent of \rho\in[0,1] (6.75) \displaystyle\xi\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho)\xi\leqslant\xi\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho;\Phi_{L})\xi\leqslant\xi\cdot\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)\xi+CL^{-\gamma_{3}}|\xi|^{2}. We take the first one and the third one, and let L\nearrow\infty , and obtain the desired result \displaystyle\mathbf{c}(\rho)\leqslant\lim_{L\to\infty}\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})=\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho). Step 2: \mathbf{c}(\rho)\geqslant\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) . We pick F_{K} as a sequence of local functions to approximate \mathbf{c}(\rho) , and compare v_{p,K}=\ell_{p}+\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}p\cdot\tau_{x}F_{K} in the functional of \overline{\nu}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda_{L},q) \displaystyle\overline{\nu}_{*}(\rho,\Lambda_{L},q) \displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}q\cdot{\overbracket{D}}_{*}^{-1}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})\cdot q \displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{2\chi(\rho)|\Lambda|}\sum_{b\in\overline{{\Lambda}^{*}}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}v_{p,K})-\frac{1}{2}c_{b}(\pi_{b}v_{p,K})^{2}}\right\rangle_{\rho}. Here we pick F_{K}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}(\Lambda_{L}) . Applying the stationarity of mapping in ( 6.69 ) and ( 3.9 ), we obtain that \displaystyle\frac{1}{2}q\cdot{\overbracket{D}}_{*}^{-1}(\rho,\Lambda_{L})q\geqslant p\cdot q-\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)}p\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho;F_{K})p. Letting L,K\to\infty , this yields \displaystyle\frac{1}{2}q\cdot{\overbracket{D}}^{-1}(\rho)q\geqslant p\cdot q-\frac{1}{4\chi(\rho)}p\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho)p. By taking q={\overbracket{D}}(\rho)p and recall 2\chi(\rho){\overbracket{D}}(\rho)=\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) from ( 5.2 ), we conclude \mathbf{c}(\rho)\geqslant\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) . Step 3: Error estimate. Once we identify that \mathbf{c}(\rho)=\overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) , we go back to ( 6.75 ) to obtain \displaystyle\xi\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho)\xi\leqslant\xi\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho;\Phi_{L})\xi\leqslant\xi\cdot\mathbf{c}(\rho)\xi+CL^{-\gamma_{3}}|\xi|^{2}. Since C,\gamma_{3} come from Proposition 6.6 and are independent of \rho , this concludes the estimate ( 6.74 ) by setting \gamma:=\gamma_{3} . ∎ 6.5. Proof of Theorem 1.3 and Theorem 1.5 We summarize the proof of the main theorems in this paper. Proof of Theorem 1.3 and Theorem 1.5 . The finite-volume approximation defined in ( 1.25 ) gives a subadditive quantity \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho,\Lambda_{L}) , which defines a limit \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) in ( 5.2 ). Using the dual quantity, we prove the convergence rate of this approximation in Proposition 5.1 and Proposition 6.4 . In Lemma 6.8 , we identify that \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho) coincides with \mathbf{c}(\rho) defined in ( 1.15 ), therefore Proposition 5.1 together with Proposition 6.4 proves Theorem 1.5 . Finally, Proposition 6.6 removes the dependence of density of the local corrector and Lemma 6.8 proves Theorem 1.3 , where ( 6.73 ) gives a concrete construction of the density-free local corrector. ∎ 6.6. Application: CLT variance in gradient replacement This subsection is an application of the results in Theorems 1.3 and 1.5 . We will calculate the CLT variance in the gradient replacement, which corresponds to [ 36 , Section 5] . Especially, the homogenization theory provides an alternative approach besides Varadhan’s characterization of closed form ( [ 36 , Theorem 4.1] ), together with a convergence rate. Let us make our setting clear. For any finite set \Lambda\subseteq{\mathbb{Z}^{d}} and M\in\mathbb{N}:0\leqslant M\leqslant|\Lambda| , we denote by \displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda}:=\{0,1\}^{\Lambda},\qquad\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda,M}:=\left\{\eta\in\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda};\sum_{x\in\Lambda}\eta_{x}=M\right\}. Using the notation ( 1.48 ), every configuration \eta\in\mathcal{X}=\{0,1\}^{{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}} can be decomposed into two parts (6.76) \displaystyle\eta=\xi\cdot\zeta,\qquad\xi:=\eta_{|\Lambda}\in\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda},\qquad\zeta:=\eta_{|\Lambda^{c}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda^{c}}. We let \mathcal{L}_{\Lambda,\zeta} be a restriction of the generator \mathcal{L} defined in ( 1.9 ) on \Lambda with the exterior condition \zeta (6.77) \displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda,\zeta}f(\xi):=\sum_{b\in\Lambda^{*}}c_{b}(\xi\cdot\zeta)\pi_{b}f(\xi), for a function f on \mathcal{X}_{\Lambda} . Let \mathbb{P}_{\Lambda,M,\zeta} be the uniform probability measure on \mathcal{X}_{\Lambda,M} given \zeta , and we denote by \langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{\Lambda,M,\zeta} the expectation with respect to \mathbb{P}_{\Lambda,M,\zeta} ; see Section 1.4.2 . Using the decomposition \eta=\xi\cdot\zeta from ( 6.76 ) on the domain \Lambda_{L}=(-L/2,L/2)^{d}\cap{\mathbb{Z}^{d}} , we define the \mathbb{R}^{d} -valued functions A_{L},B_{L,\zeta} and H_{L,\zeta,F} of \xi (so those of \eta ) as (6.78) \begin{split}A_{L}(\xi)&:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{x,y\in\Lambda_{L}:|x-y|=1}(\xi_{y}-\xi_{x})(y-x)=-\sum_{b\in\Lambda^{*}}\pi_{b}\left(\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L}}x\xi_{x}\right),\\ B_{L,\zeta}(\xi)&:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{x,y\in\Lambda_{L}:|x-y|=1}W_{x,y}(\eta)(y-x)=-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta}\Big(\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L}}x\xi_{x}\Big),\\ H_{L,\zeta,F}(\xi)&:=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L-r(F)-1}}\tau_{x}(\mathcal{L}F)(\eta)=\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta}\bigg(\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L-r(F)-1}}\tau_{x}F\bigg)(\xi).\end{split} Here B_{L,\zeta} denotes the microscopic current with (6.79) \displaystyle W_{x,y}(\eta):=c_{x,y}(\eta)(\eta_{y}-\eta_{x}), and F=(F_{i})_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{d} in H_{L,\zeta,F} will be taken as a correction function. The correction function F should be local compared to \Lambda_{L} , so we require r(F)\leqslant L-1 , where r(F) is the diameter of the support of F \displaystyle r(F):=\min\big\{r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}:F\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{d}(\Lambda_{r})\big\}. Given 0\leqslant M\leqslant|\Lambda_{L}| and \zeta\in\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{L}^{c}}=\{0,1\}^{\Lambda_{L}^{c}} , for functions f,g:\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{L},M}\to\mathbb{R} such that \left\langle{f}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta}=\left\langle{g}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta}=0 , their CLT covariance and variance are respectively defined by (6.80) \displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Delta_{L,M,\zeta}[f,g]&:=\left\langle{f(-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta})^{-1}g}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta},\\ \Delta_{L,M,\zeta}[f]&:=\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}[f,f].\end{aligned} In the gradient replacement argument, the heuristic is that, when F is well-chosen, we should have \displaystyle D(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)A_{L}\simeq(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F}), where D(\rho)=\mathbf{c}(\rho)/2\chi(\rho) is the diffusion matrix defined in ( 1.12 ). The error in this replacement is small in weak sense, so we aim to estimate the following CLT variance (6.81) Q_{L}(F;q,M,\zeta)\\ :=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\left[q\cdot\{D(M/|\Lambda_{L}|)A_{L}-(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F})\}\right]-\frac{1}{2}q\cdot R(M/|\Lambda_{L}|;F)q, with q\in B_{1} and R(\rho;F)=\mathbf{c}(\rho;F)-\mathbf{c}(\rho) as the error defined in ( 1.20 ). Proposition 6.10 below provides the convergence rate of Q_{L}(F;q,M,\zeta) , which is a quantitative refinement of the result shown in [ 36 , Corollary 5.1] and is a key for proving the hydrodynamic limit for non-gradient models. Actually, the quantity Q_{L}(F;q,M,\zeta) can be divided into three pieces: (6.82) \begin{split}Q^{(a)}_{L}(\widetilde{q},M,\zeta)&:=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\left[\widetilde{q}\cdot A_{L}\right]-2(\widetilde{q}\cdot{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}(\hat{\rho})\widetilde{q})\chi^{2}(\hat{\rho}),\\ Q_{L}^{(b)}(F;q,M,\zeta)&:=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\left[q\cdot(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F})\right]-\frac{1}{2}q\cdot{\mathbf{c}}(\hat{\rho};F)q,\\ Q_{L}^{(c)}(F;q,\widetilde{q},M,\zeta)&:=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\left[\widetilde{q}\cdot A_{L},q\cdot(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F})\right]-(q\cdot\widetilde{q})\chi(\hat{\rho}),\end{split} with the shorthand notation \hat{\rho}:=M/|\Lambda_{L}| , for the empirical density. Proposition 6.10 . By choosing \widetilde{q}=D(\hat{\rho})q in ( 6.82 ), the following identity is valid for Q_{L}(F;q,M,\zeta) (6.83) Q_{L}(F;q,M,\zeta)\\ =Q^{(a)}_{L}(D(\hat{\rho})q,M,\zeta)+Q_{L}^{(b)}(F;q,M,\zeta)-2Q_{L}^{(c)}(F;q,D(\hat{\rho})q,M,\zeta). Moreover, there exists a finite positive constant C=C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) such that the quantity Q_{L}(F;q,M,\zeta) defined in ( 6.81 ) satisfies the following estimate for all L\in\mathbb{N}_{+} and F\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{d} (6.84) Q_{L}(F):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in B_{1},0\leqslant M\leqslant|\Lambda_{L}|,\\ \zeta\in\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{L}^{c}}\end{subarray}}|Q_{L}(F;q,M,\zeta)|\\ \leqslant C\Big(L^{-\gamma_{2}}+r(F)^{d}(1+r(F)^{2d}\|F\|_{\infty}^{2})L^{-1}+r(F)^{2d}\|F\|_{\infty}L^{-d}+L^{-1}\Big). Here the positive exponent \gamma_{2} is the same one as in Proposition 6.4 and Theorem 1.5 . We study at first the error estimate for each term in ( 6.83 ). 6.6.1. CLT variance of A_{L} For the CLT variance of A_{L} , it was shown in Theorem 5.1 of [ 36 ] that (6.85) \displaystyle\lim_{L,M\to\infty,M/|\Lambda_{L}|\to\rho}|\Lambda_{L}|^{-1}\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}(\widetilde{q}\cdot A_{L})=2(\widetilde{q}\cdot\mathbf{c}^{-1}(\rho)\widetilde{q})\chi^{2}(\rho), uniformly in \rho\in[0,1] and \eta\in\mathcal{X} ; recall \zeta=\eta|_{\Lambda_{L}^{c}} . This is the key in the gradient replacement and shown based on a characterization of closed forms given in Corollary 4.1 of [ 36 ] . The computation of Q_{L}^{(a)} , as the error in ( 6.85 ), is indeed deeply related to the dual quantity studied in ( 6.14 ), and this makes Varadhan’s lemma avoidable. It is, in a sense, hidden in the variational formula for \widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M) . In other words, our dual computation well fits to computing the CLT variance of A_{L} . This matches with the dual computation in (5.7) in the proof of Theorem 5.1 in [ 36 ] , but our computation is presented at higher level on the configuration space. We can write the error Q_{L}^{(a)} of the CLT variance of A_{L} defined by ( 6.82 ) concretely in terms of the dual quantity as in ( 6.88 ) below. It is important that we can give such an exact formula before taking the limit so that one can directly apply our estimate to obtain its convergence rate. Note that, to prove the hydrodynamic limit formulated in Theorem 1.4 , we need the uniformity in the density \rho\in[0,1] as in Corollary 5.1 in [ 36 ] . The decay estimate for Q_{L}^{(a)}(q,M,\zeta) is stated as follows. This is one of the main achievements of this paper. Proposition 6.11 . There exists a finite positive constant C=C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) such that (6.86) \displaystyle\sup_{q\in B_{1},0\leqslant M\leqslant|\Lambda_{L}|,\zeta\in\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{L}^{c}}}|Q_{L}^{(a)}(q,M,\zeta)|\leqslant CL^{-\gamma_{2}}. Here the positive exponent \gamma_{2} is the same one as in Proposition 6.4 and Theorem 1.5 . Proof. Let us implement the discussion in previous sections to Q_{L}^{(a)} . From ( 6.78 ) and ( 1.50 ), the quantity q\cdot A_{L} satisfies the identity \displaystyle q\cdot A_{L}=-\sum_{b=\{x,y\}\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}\pi_{b}\ell_{q}. Then by variational formula of the dual quantity \widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M) defined in ( 6.14 ), the optimizer u(\Lambda_{L},q) satisfies that \displaystyle\sum_{b\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}c_{b}\pi_{b}u(\Lambda_{L},q)=\sum_{b\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}\pi_{b}\ell_{q}. Recall the generator \mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta} defined in ( 6.77 ), thus the quantity (-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta})^{-1}(q\cdot A_{L}) is nothing but u(\Lambda_{L},q) defined in (1) of Proposition 4.1 (see also Remark 4.2 ) because of the following identity 1 1 1 Here we abuse the notation because the generator defined in ( 6.77 ), i.e. (5.1) in [ 36 ] , is slightly different from ( 1.26 ). Nevertheless, this tiny difference does no harm; see Remark 1.6 . (6.87) \displaystyle(-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta})^{-1}(q\cdot A_{L})=(\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta})^{-1}\left(\sum_{b\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}\pi_{b}\ell_{q}\right)=u(\Lambda_{L},q). Therefore, we put this result back to \Delta_{L,M,\zeta}[q\cdot A_{L}] and obtain \displaystyle\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}[q\cdot A_{L}] \displaystyle=\left\langle{(q\cdot A_{L})(-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta})^{-1}(q\cdot A_{L})}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta} \displaystyle=\left\langle{(q\cdot A_{L})u(\Lambda_{L},q)}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta} \displaystyle=-\sum_{b\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})u(\Lambda_{L},q)}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta} \displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{b\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}u(\Lambda_{L},q))}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta} \displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{b\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}\left\langle{c_{b}(\pi_{b}u(\Lambda_{L},q))^{2}}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta}. Here from the third line to the fourth line, we use the identity \pi_{b}\pi_{b}=-2\pi_{b} and integration by part. From the fourth line to the fifth line, we use the variational formula of \widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M) once again rewritten similar to ( 4.3 ). This concludes that \displaystyle|\Lambda_{L}|^{-1}\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}[q\cdot A_{L}]=\left(2\chi(\hat{\rho})\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}q\cdot\widehat{D}^{-1}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)q\right)=\chi(\hat{\rho})q\cdot\widehat{D}^{-1}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)q. We put this result back to ( 6.82 ), and obtain that (6.88) \displaystyle Q^{(a)}_{L}(q,M,\zeta) \displaystyle=\chi(\hat{\rho})q\cdot\left(\widehat{D}^{-1}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)-D^{-1}(\hat{\rho})\right)q \displaystyle=\chi(\hat{\rho})q\cdot\widehat{D}^{-1}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)\left(D(\hat{\rho})-\widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)\right)D^{-1}(\hat{\rho})q \displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}q\cdot\widehat{D}^{-1}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)\left(\mathbf{c}(\hat{\rho})-\hat{\mathbf{c}}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M)\right)D^{-1}(\hat{\rho})q. Apply the quantitative homogenization of canonical ensemble in ( 1.30 ) (see also Proposition 6.4 and \overline{\mathbf{c}}(\rho)=\mathbf{c}(\rho) proved in Proposition 6.9 ), noting the uniform positivity of matrices \widehat{D}_{*}(\Lambda_{L},M) and D(\hat{\rho}) in (2) of Proposition 4.1 , we obtain the desired result ( 6.86 ). ∎ 6.6.2. CLT variance of B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F} and covariance between A_{L} and B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F} The quantitative estimates of Q_{L}^{(b)} and Q_{L}^{(c)} are given in the following lemma, which is a refinement of Proposition 5.1 of [ 36 ] . Lemma 6.12 . There exists a finite positive constant C=C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) such that (6.89) \displaystyle|Q_{L}^{(b)}(F;q,M,\zeta)|\leqslant Cr(F)^{d}(1+r(F)^{2d}\|F\|_{\infty}^{2})L^{-1}, (6.90) \displaystyle|Q_{L}^{(c)}(F;q,\widetilde{q},M,\zeta)|\leqslant Cr(F)^{2d}\|F\|_{\infty}L^{-d}+CL^{-1}, uniformly in q,\widetilde{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|q|=|\widetilde{q}|=1 , 0\leqslant M\leqslant|\Lambda_{L}| and \zeta\in\mathcal{X}_{\Lambda_{L}^{c}} . Proof. The proof of this lemma is exactly the same as that of Proposition 5.2 of [ 34 ] , which discusses the Kawasaki dynamics adding the effect of creation and annihilation of particles called the Glauber effect, but the Glauber part does not play any role in its proof. We give a sketch of it here. Actually, we can consider a function (6.91) \displaystyle v(\Lambda_{L},q,F):=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L}}(q\cdot x)\xi_{x}+\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{L-r(F)-1}}q\cdot\tau_{x}F, then the term B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F} can be represented as \displaystyle q\cdot(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F})=-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta}v(\Lambda_{L},q,F). We can thus reformulate Q_{L}^{(b)}(F;q,M,\zeta) in ( 6.82 ) as \displaystyle Q_{L}^{(b)}(F;q,M,\zeta)=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{v(\Lambda_{L},q,F)\left(-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta}v(\Lambda_{L},q,F)\right)}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta}-\frac{1}{2}q\cdot{\mathbf{c}}(\hat{\rho};F)q. Then the calculation is quite similar as the subadditive quantity in ( 1.25 ), and the error comes from the local equivalence of ensembles and the boundary layer. Concerning the term Q_{L}^{(c)}(F;q,\widetilde{q},M,\zeta) , we also have a representation using ( 6.91 ) and ( 6.87 ) \displaystyle Q_{L}^{(c)}(F;q,\widetilde{q},M,\zeta) \displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\left\langle{u(\Lambda_{L},q)\left(-\mathcal{L}_{\Lambda_{L},\zeta}v(\Lambda_{L},\widetilde{q},F)\right)}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta}-(q\cdot\widetilde{q})\chi(\hat{\rho}) \displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{b\in(\Lambda_{L})^{*}}\left\langle{(\pi_{b}\ell_{q})(\pi_{b}v(\Lambda_{L},\widetilde{q},F))}\right\rangle_{\Lambda_{L},M,\zeta}-(q\cdot\widetilde{q})\chi(\hat{\rho}). The second line comes from the variational formula of u(\Lambda_{L},q) ; see ( 4.1 ). Since v(\Lambda_{L},\widetilde{q},F)\in\ell_{q,\Lambda_{L}}+\mathcal{F}_{0}^{-}(\Lambda_{L}) , the first term will yield (q\cdot\widetilde{q})\chi(\hat{\rho}) under \mathbb{P}_{\hat{\rho}} ; see ( 3.9 ). The error just comes from the local equivalence of ensembles. ∎ 6.6.3. CLT variance of D(\hat{\rho})A_{L}-(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F}) Proposition 6.10 is shown by combining Proposition 6.11 and Lemma 6.12 , and gives a quantitative refinement of Corollary 5.1 of [ 36 ] . Proof of Proposition 6.10 . Omitting \hat{\rho} , we simply write D,\mathbf{c},\mathbf{c}(F) , R(F) and \chi for D(\hat{\rho}) , \mathbf{c}(\hat{\rho}) , \mathbf{c}(\hat{\rho};F) , R(\hat{\rho};F) and \chi(\hat{\rho}) , respectively. Then, noting that D is symmetric, we have \displaystyle|\Lambda_{L} \displaystyle|^{-1}\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\big[q\cdot\{DA_{L}-(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F})\}\big] \displaystyle=|\Lambda_{L}|^{-1}\Big\{\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\big[Dq\cdot A_{L}\big]-2\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\big[Dq\cdot A_{L},q\cdot(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F})\big] \displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\Delta_{L,M,\zeta}\big[q\cdot(B_{L,\zeta}-H_{L,\zeta,F})\big]\Big\} \displaystyle=\big\{Q_{L}^{(a)}(Dq,M,\zeta)+2\big(Dq\cdot\mathbf{c}^{-1}Dq\big)\,\chi^{2}\big\}-2\big\{Q_{L}^{(c)}(F;q,Dq,M,\zeta)+\big(q\cdot Dq\big)\,\chi\big\} \displaystyle\quad+\big\{Q_{L}^{(b)}(F;q,M,\zeta)+\frac{1}{2}\big(q\cdot\mathbf{c}(F)q\big)\big\}. However, by D=\mathbf{c}/2\chi and \mathbf{c}(F)-\mathbf{c}=R(F) , the term except for Q^{(a)},Q^{(b)},Q^{(c)} on the right-hand side is rewritten as \displaystyle 2\big(Dq\cdot\mathbf{c}^{-1}Dq\big)\,\chi^{2}-2\big(q\cdot Dq\big)\,\chi+\frac{1}{2}\big(q\cdot\mathbf{c}(F)q\big) \displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}q\cdot\mathbf{c}q-q\cdot\mathbf{c}q+\frac{1}{2}\big(q\cdot\mathbf{c}(F)q\big) \displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}q\cdot R(F)q. This proves the identity ( 6.83 ). Afterwards, the bound for Q_{L}(F) follows from ( 6.86 ) (combined with the boundedness of D(\rho) ), ( 6.89 ) and ( 6.90 ) by changing the constant C>0 if necessary. ∎ 7. Quantitative hydrodynamic limit The main task of this section is to establish a quantitative hydrodynamic limit for non-gradient models. We introduce the settings in Section 7.1 , then reduce Theorem 1.4 to the relative entropy in Section 7.2 . The main part of the proof follows [ 36 ] , and we reformulate a self-contained version in Section 7.3 . The main improvement is a quantitative version of gradient replacement, which does not require the characterization of the closed forms (Varadhan’s lemma), but brings a convergence rate. Some preparation about the CLT variance is done in Section 6.6 . 7.1. Setting of hydrodynamic limit 7.1.1. Dynamics As briefly explained in Section 1.1, the hydrodynamic limit was studied in [ 36 ] for the non-gradient Kawasaki dynamics on the lattice torus \mathbb{T}_{N}^{d} . It is described as a Markov process \eta^{N}(t)=\{\eta_{x}^{N}(t),x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}\} on the configuration space \mathcal{X}_{N}=\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}} , governed by the infinitesimal generator \displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{N}=N^{2}\mathcal{L}, where \mathcal{L} is the operator defined by ( 1.9 ) replacing {\mathbb{Z}}^{d} with \mathbb{T}_{N}^{d} , i.e., (7.1) \displaystyle\mathcal{L}:=\sum_{b\in(\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d})^{*}}c_{b}(\eta)\pi_{b}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}:|x-y|=1}c_{x,y}(\eta)\pi_{x,y}. The hydrodynamic limit aims to study the asymptotic behavior as N\to\infty of the macroscopic empirical mass distribution \rho^{N}(t,{\mathrm{d}}v) of \eta^{N}(t) defined by ( 1.10 ), i.e., \displaystyle\rho^{N}(t,{\mathrm{d}}v)=N^{-d}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}_{N}}\eta^{N}_{x}(t)\delta_{x/N}({\mathrm{d}}v), for v\in\mathbb{T}^{d} , and the nonlinear diffusion equation ( 1.11 ), i.e., \displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho(t,v)=\nabla\cdot(D(\rho(t,v))\nabla\rho(t,v)),\qquad(t,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}, was derived in the limit. We always assume Hypothesis 1.1 for the jump rates \{c_{b}=c_{x,y}\} and Hypothesis 1.2 for the initial value \rho_{0} of ( 1.11 ), given in Section 1 . 7.1.2. Minimizing sequence \{\Phi_{n}\} for \mathbf{c}(\rho) The following proposition is a summary from the results in Lemma 4.4 and Proposition 6.9 (see also Theorem 1.3 ). This proposition is a refinement of ( 1.21 ), originally shown in [ 36 ] . Recall the variational formula ( 1.15 ) for \mathbf{c}(\rho) and ( 1.20 ) for R(\rho;F) . We denote by r(F) the diameter of support of F that, (7.2) \displaystyle r(F):=\min\big\{L\in\mathbb{N}_{+}:F\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{d}(\Lambda_{L})\big\}. Proposition 7.1 . There exists an exponent \gamma(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})>0 (same as in Theorem 1.3 ) and a positive constant C(d,\lambda,\mathbf{r})<\infty , and a sequence of local functions \Phi_{n}(\eta)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{d} on \mathcal{X} , such that (7.3) r(\Phi_{n})\leqslant n,\quad\sup_{\rho\in[0,1]}|R(\rho;\Phi_{n})|\leqslant Cn^{-\gamma},\quad\|\Phi_{n}\|_{\infty}\leqslant Cn^{2}\log n. 7.1.3. Relative entropy and local equilibrium state Let h_{N}(f|\psi) be the relative entropy per volume for two probability densities f and \psi with respect to \nu^{N}\equiv\nu^{N}_{1/2} defined as (7.4) \displaystyle h_{N}(f|\psi):=N^{-d}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{N}}f\log(f/\psi)\,{\mathrm{d}}\nu^{N}. Here \nu_{1/2}^{N} is the Bernoulli product measure of parameter \frac{1}{2} on \mathcal{X}_{N} . The local equilibrium state of second order approximation \psi_{t}^{N} is defined by (7.5) \displaystyle\psi^{N}_{t}(\eta) \displaystyle\equiv\psi_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}}^{N}(\eta) \displaystyle:=\frac{1}{Z_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}}}\exp\bigg\{\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\lambda(t,x/N)\eta_{x}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\nabla\lambda(t,x/N)\cdot\tau_{x}F_{N}(\eta)\bigg\}, where Z_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}} is the normalization constant with respect to the Bernoulli product measure \nu^{N} , and \nabla\lambda=(\partial_{v_{i}}\lambda)_{i=1}^{d} is the spatial gradient, and the local function F_{N}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{d} serves a correction term. Let f_{t}^{N} stand for the density of the distribution of \eta^{N}(t) on \mathcal{X}_{N} under \nu^{N} , and we consider its relative entropy with respect to the corrected local equilibrium state (7.6) h_{N}(t):=h_{N}(f^{N}_{t}|\psi^{N}_{t}). Recall ( 7.4 ) for the definition of h_{N}(\cdot|\cdot) . The density \psi_{t}^{N} can be seen as a good local equilibrium approximation of \rho(t,v) only when the functions \lambda(t,v) and F_{N} are well-chosen. Let \overline{\lambda}:(0,1)\to\mathbb{R} be a function defined by (7.7) \overline{\lambda}(\rho)=\log\{\rho/(1-\rho)\},\quad\rho\in(0,1). We also consider \overline{\rho}:\mathbb{R}\to(0,1) as the inverse function of the mapping \rho\mapsto\overline{\lambda}(\rho) , i.e., (7.8) \overline{\rho}(\lambda)=e^{\lambda}/(e^{\lambda}+1),\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{R}. Condition 7.2 . Recall the solution \rho(t,v) of the hydrodynamic equation ( 1.11 ) and \Phi_{n} defined in Proposition 7.1 . We define the following choice of functions (7.9) \displaystyle\lambda(t,v)=\overline{\lambda}(\rho(t,v)),\qquad and (7.10) \displaystyle F_{N}=\Phi_{n(N)},\qquad n(N):=\lfloor N^{s_{1}}\rfloor,\qquad s_{1}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{8(d+2)^{2}}\right). Recall Hypothesis 1.2 for the initial value \rho_{0} of the equation ( 1.11 ) (i.e., smooth and 0<\rho_{0}(v)<1 ) assumed for Theorem 1.4 . Then, by the maximum principle, we have 0<\rho(t,v)<1 ; see below Hypothesis 1.2 . Therefore, \lambda(t,v) is well-defined. Roughly, F_{N} aims to minimize \sup_{\rho\in[0,1]}|R(\rho;\Phi_{n})| , and its support is mesoscopic compared to N . The choice of scale s_{1} will be clear later (see ( 7.63 ) below). In the following paragraphs, because several estimates are more general, we usually assume the approximation \psi_{t}^{N} without specific choices of \lambda(t,v),F_{N} . The Condition 7.2 enters the proof in the end. 7.1.4. Probability measures Following the convention \psi^{N}_{t}(\eta)\equiv\psi_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}}^{N}(\eta) in ( 7.5 ), we identify \psi_{t,0}^{N}=\psi_{\lambda(t,\cdot),0}^{N} by taking F_{N}=0 . Then we define the following probability measures on \mathcal{X}_{N} , (7.11) \displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}P^{\psi_{t}^{N}}:=\psi_{t}^{N}\,{\mathrm{d}}\nu^{N},\qquad{\mathrm{d}}P^{\psi_{t,0}^{N}}:=\psi_{t,0}^{N}{\mathrm{d}}\nu^{N},\qquad P\equiv P^{f_{t}^{N}}:=f_{t}^{N}\,{\mathrm{d}}\nu^{N}, and the expectations under them by E^{\psi_{t}^{N}} , E^{\psi_{t,0}^{N}} and E\equiv E^{f_{t}^{N}} , respectively. Notice that the measure P and the expectation E are canonical as they are associated to (\eta^{N}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0} . 7.1.5. Sobolev spaces on \mathbb{T}^{d} Let us recall the Sobolev spaces H^{\alpha}\equiv H^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) on \mathbb{T}^{d} for \alpha\in\mathbb{R} . Associated with the Laplacian \Delta on \mathbb{T}^{d} , we have eigenfunctions e_{\bf n} of -\Delta indexed by {\bf n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{d})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d} \displaystyle-\Delta e_{\bf n}=\lambda_{\bf n}e_{\bf n},\qquad\lambda_{\bf n}:=\pi^{2}|{\bf n}|^{2}=\pi^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}n_{i}^{2}. Let \{e_{\bf n}\}_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}} form a complete orthonormal system of L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) ; see Section 2.2 of [ 34 ] for the concrete expression of e_{\bf n} using the trigonometric functions. Then, the norm \|g\|_{H^{\alpha}} is defined first for g\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) by (7.12) \displaystyle\|g\|_{H^{\alpha}}^{2}:=\langle(-\Delta+1)^{\alpha}g,g\rangle=\sum_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}(\lambda_{\bf n}+1)^{\alpha}\langle g,e_{\bf n}\rangle^{2}, where \langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle denotes the inner product of L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) . The Sobolev space H^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) is the completion of C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) under the norm \|\cdot\|_{H^{\alpha}} . 7.2. Hydrodynamic limit via relative entropy As shown in [ 36 ] , the key to prove the hydrodynamic limit is the decay of h_{N}(t) defined in ( 7.6 ). Proposition 7.3 . Under Condition 7.2 , we assume that h_{N}(0)\leqslant C_{0}N^{-\kappa_{0}} for some C_{0}>0 and \kappa_{0}>0 , and Hypothesis 1.2 for the initial value \rho_{0} . Then, for every T>0 , there exist two finite positive constants C_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}(C_{0},T,\rho_{0},\kappa_{0},d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) and \kappa_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}(\kappa_{0},d,\lambda,\mathbf{r}) such that (7.13) \displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T],\qquad h_{N}(t)\leqslant C_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}}. The proof of Proposition 7.3 is postponed to Section 7.3 . In this part, we admit it and deduce Theorem 1.4 via the concentration inequality and the entropy inequality. We prepare at first two lemmas. Lemma 7.4 . Under the same setting as Theorem 1.4 , for every T>0 , there exist two finite positive constants C_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}(C_{0},\kappa_{0},T) and \kappa_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}(C_{0},\kappa_{0}) such that \sup_{t\in[0,T]}E\Big[|\langle\rho^{N}(t),\phi\rangle-\langle\rho(t),\phi\rangle_{N}|^{2}\Big]\leqslant C_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}\|\phi\|_{\infty}^{2}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}}, where the two inner products are defined as (7.14) \begin{split}\langle\rho(t),\phi\rangle_{N}&:=\frac{1}{N^{d}}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\rho(t,x/N)\phi(x/N),\\ \langle\rho^{N}(t),\phi\rangle&:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\phi(v)\rho^{N}(t,{\mathrm{d}}v).\end{split} Proof. By the entropy inequality [ 54 , Appendix 1.8] , we have (7.15) E\Big[|\langle\rho^{N}(t),\phi\rangle-\langle\rho(t),\phi\rangle_{N}|^{2}\Big]\leqslant\frac{4}{N^{d}}\log E^{\psi_{t,0}^{N}}\Big[e^{X_{N}^{2}/4}\Big]+4h_{N}(f_{t}^{N}|\psi_{t,0}^{N}), where X_{N} is defined by \displaystyle X_{N}:=N^{d/2}\{\langle\rho^{N},\phi\rangle-\langle\rho(t),\phi\rangle_{N}\}=N^{-d/2}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\zeta_{x}\phi(x/N), and \zeta_{x}:=\eta_{x}-\rho(t,x/N) . To estimate the entropy in ( 7.15 ), we compare \psi_{t}^{N}=\psi_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}}^{N} and \psi_{t,0}^{N}=\psi_{\lambda(t,\cdot),0}^{N} recalling ( 7.5 ): \psi_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}}^{N}=\frac{Z_{\lambda(t,\cdot),0}}{Z_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}}}e^{R(\eta)}\psi_{\lambda(t,\cdot),0}^{N}, where R(\eta)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\nabla\lambda(t,x/N)\cdot\tau_{x}F_{N}(\eta). We have a simple bound (7.16) \displaystyle|R(\eta)|\leqslant N^{d-1}\|\nabla\lambda(t)||_{\infty}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{2}N^{d-\kappa_{1}}, for some 0<\kappa_{1}<1 (indeed, \kappa_{1}=1-2s_{1}-\varepsilon,\varepsilon>0 ), by \sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\nabla\lambda(t)\|_{\infty}<\infty and Proposition 7.1 . Recall that F_{N}=\Phi_{n(N)} with the choice of parameters in ( 7.10 ). This also implies that \displaystyle 0<\frac{Z_{\lambda(t,\cdot),0}}{Z_{\lambda(t,\cdot),F_{N}}}\leqslant e^{\|R\|_{\infty}}. Thus, in ( 7.15 ), we can estimate it as (7.17) \begin{split}h_{N}(f_{t}^{N}|\psi_{t,0}^{N})&=N^{-d}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{N}}\Big(\log\frac{f_{t}^{N}}{\psi_{t}^{N}}+\log\frac{\psi_{t}^{N}}{\psi_{t,0}^{N}}\Big)f_{t}^{N}d\nu^{N}\\ &\leqslant h_{N}(f_{t}^{N}|\psi_{t}^{N})+2N^{-d}\|R\|_{\infty}\\ &\leqslant C_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}}+2C_{2}N^{-\kappa_{1}}.\end{split} In the last line, we make use of the the assumption h_{N}(0)\leqslant C_{0}N^{-\kappa_{0}} in Theorem 1.4 , together with Proposition 7.3 . On the other hand, for the first term in ( 7.15 ), since \{\zeta_{x}\} are independent and mean 0 under P^{\psi_{t,0}^{N}} , by Hoeffding’s inequality (see Theorem 2.8 in [ 18 ] , take N^{d/2}s for t and note |\zeta_{x}\phi(\tfrac{x}{N})|\leqslant\|\phi\|_{\infty} ), we obtain that \displaystyle P^{\psi_{t,0}^{N}}\big(|X_{N}|\geqslant s\big)\leqslant 2e^{-s^{2}/(2\|\phi\|_{\infty}^{2})}, for all s>0 and \phi\not\equiv 0 . Thus, we have (7.18) \displaystyle E^{\psi_{t,0}^{N}}\Big[e^{X_{N}^{2}/4}\Big] \displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}E^{\psi_{t,0}^{N}}\Big[e^{X_{N}^{2}/4};i\leqslant|X_{N}|<i+1\Big] \displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}e^{(i+1)^{2}/4}2e^{-i^{2}/(2\|\phi\|_{\infty}^{2})}=:C_{3}<\infty, uniformly for \phi with \|\phi\|_{\infty}=1 . Summarizing ( 7.15 ), ( 7.17 ), ( 7.18 ), we have E\Big[|\langle\rho^{N}(t),\phi\rangle-\langle\rho(t),\phi\rangle_{N}|^{2}\Big]\leqslant\frac{4\log C_{3}}{N^{d}}+4\big(C_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}}+2C_{2}N^{-\kappa_{1}}\big)\leqslant C_{4}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}\wedge\kappa_{1}}, where the constant C_{4}=C_{4,T}>0 is uniform in \phi such that \|\phi\|_{\infty}=1 . For general \phi(\not\equiv 0) , taking \phi/\|\phi\|_{\infty} for \phi , we obtain E\Big[|\langle\rho^{N}(t),\phi\rangle-\langle\rho(t),\phi\rangle_{N}|^{2}\Big]\leqslant C_{4}\|\phi\|_{\infty}^{2}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}}, by writing \kappa_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}:=\kappa_{\ref{Corollary 2.1}}\wedge\kappa_{1} . This concludes the proof of the lemma. ∎ Next, for \beta\in(0,1] , let us consider the Hölder norms \|g\|_{C^{\beta}}=\|g\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})} of a function g on \mathbb{T}^{d} : \|g\|_{C^{\beta}}:=\|g\|_{\infty}+\sup_{v_{1}\not=v_{2}}\frac{|g(v_{1})-g(v_{2})|}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|^{\beta}}, where |v|\in[0,\sqrt{d}/2] is the Euclidean norm defined for v\in\mathbb{T}^{d} modulo 1 in each component. The next lemma is elementary and we refer to [ 34 , Lemma 2.3] for its proof. Lemma 7.5 . For every \beta\in(0,1] and functions \rho,\phi\in C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) , we have |\langle\rho,\phi\rangle_{N}-\langle\rho,\phi\rangle|\leqslant d^{\beta/2}(2N)^{-\beta}\|\rho\cdot\phi\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}. We are at the position to give the proof of Theorem 1.4 . Proof of Theorem 1.4 via Proposition 7.3 . By ( 7.12 ), one can rewrite and estimate as (7.19) \displaystyle E\Big[\|\rho^{N}(t)-\rho(t)\|_{H^{-\alpha}}^{2}\Big] \displaystyle=\sum_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}(\lambda_{\bf n}+1)^{-\alpha}E\Big[\langle\rho^{N}(t)-\rho(t),e_{\bf n}\rangle^{2}\Big] \displaystyle\leqslant 2\sum_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}(\lambda_{\bf n}+1)^{-\alpha}E\Big[\big(\langle\rho^{N}(t),e_{\bf n}\rangle-\langle\rho(t),e_{\bf n}\rangle_{N}\big)^{2}\Big] \displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}(\lambda_{\bf n}+1)^{-\alpha}\big(\langle\rho(t),e_{\bf n}\rangle_{N}-\langle\rho(t),e_{\bf n}\rangle\big)^{2}. By Lemma 7.4 and noting \|e_{\bf n}\|_{\infty}\leqslant(\sqrt{2})^{d} for all {\bf n} , the first term on the right-hand side of ( 7.19 ) is bounded by (7.20) \displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}}\sum_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}(\lambda_{\bf n}+1)^{-\alpha}\leqslant C_{2}N^{-\kappa_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}}, with C_{2}=C_{2,T,\alpha}>0 if \alpha>d/2 . On the other hand, by Lemma 7.5 , the second term on the right-hand side of ( 7.19 ) is bounded by (7.21) \displaystyle 2\sum_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}(\lambda_{\bf n}+1)^{-\alpha}d^{\beta}(2N)^{-2\beta}\|\rho(t)e_{\bf n}\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}, for every \beta\in(0,1] . However, since \|\rho(t)e_{\bf n}\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leqslant\|\rho(t)\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\|e_{\bf n}\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})} , \displaystyle\|e_{\bf n}\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leqslant\|e_{\bf n}\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla e_{\bf n}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}(2\|e_{\bf n}\|_{\infty})^{1-\beta}\leqslant C_{5}(|{\bf n}|^{\beta}+1), and \sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\rho(t)\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}<\infty due to the smoothness of \rho(t) , the series ( 7.21 ) is bounded by (7.22) \displaystyle C_{6}N^{-2\beta}\sum_{{\bf n}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}}(\lambda_{\bf n}+1)^{-\alpha}(|{\bf n}|^{\beta}+1)^{2}\leqslant C_{7}N^{-2\beta}, if \alpha>\beta+d/2 holds. Notice the assumption \alpha>d/2 , we can find \beta>0 small enough satisfying this condition. Thus, from ( 7.19 ), ( 7.20 ) and ( 7.22 ), we obtain the conclusion of Theorem 1.4 by taking \kappa=\kappa_{\ref{lem:Add-2}}\wedge 2\beta with \beta\in(0,(\alpha-d/2)\wedge 1) . ∎ Our method and estimates also apply to the non-gradient Glauber–Kawasaki dynamics. This is discussed in [ 34 ] . 7.3. Decay of relative entropy We prove Proposition 7.3 in this subsection. The sketch follows [ 36 ] , and we outline the procedure with the highlight of improvement. The main idea of proof is to calculate the time derivative \partial_{t}h_{N}(t) . It is done in Section 7.3.1 , then the error bound is discussed in the the following paragraphs, which mainly consists of 3 parts. • Section 7.3.2 implements a refined one-block estimate, where the functions are evaluated using the local particle density. • Section 7.3.3 is devoted to the gradient replacement. This part is the main improvement, and makes use of the results from homogenization. Especially, the inputs are Theorem 1.3 (Proposition 7.1 ) and the CLT variance in Proposition 6.10 . • Section 7.3.4 studies the error of large deviation. Finally, Gronwall’s inequality is applied in Section 7.3.5 , and the parameters of several mesoscopic scales are well chosen to conclude the proof. The proofs of several lemmas and proposition are postponed to Appendix C . Note that “the two-blocks estimate” is unnecessary in the relative entropy method. 7.3.1. Time derivative of the relative entropy h_{N}(t) We follow at first [ 36 , Lemma 3.1] to develop the time derivative \partial_{t}h_{N}(t) . The error was of order o(1) , and we aim to refine it the present work. Recall \nabla\lambda=(\partial_{v_{i}}\lambda)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant d} . Moreover, we denote (7.23) \displaystyle\nabla^{2}\lambda=(\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{v_{j}}\lambda)_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant d},\qquad\nabla^{3}\lambda=(\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{v_{j}}\partial_{v_{k}}\lambda)_{1\leqslant i,j,k\leqslant d},\qquad\dot{\lambda}=\partial\lambda/\partial t. and set (7.24) \displaystyle\|\nabla\lambda\|_{3,\infty}:=\|\nabla\lambda\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla^{2}\lambda\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla^{3}\lambda\|_{\infty}. Lemma 7.6 . Assume \lambda\in C^{1,3}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}) , then we have (7.25) \displaystyle\partial_{t}h_{N}(t)\leqslant E^{f^{N}_{t}}[\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}]+N^{-d}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\dot{\lambda}(t,x/N)\rho(t,x/N)+Q_{N}^{En}(\lambda,F_{N}), where \Omega_{1}=\Omega_{1}(\eta) and \Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}(\eta) are defined respectively by (7.26) \displaystyle\Omega_{1}(\eta):= \displaystyle-\frac{N^{1-d}}{2}\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}:x\sim y}c_{x,y}\Omega_{x,y}, (7.27) \displaystyle\Omega_{2}(\eta):= \displaystyle-N^{-d}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\dot{\lambda}(t,x/N)\eta_{x}+\frac{N^{-d}}{4}\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}:x\sim y}c_{x,y}\Omega_{x,y}^{2} \displaystyle-\frac{N^{-d}}{4}\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}:x\sim y}c_{x,y}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{v_{j}}\lambda(t,x/N)(y_{i}-x_{i})(y_{j}-x_{j})(\eta_{y}-\eta_{x}) \displaystyle+\frac{N^{-d}}{2}\sum_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}:x\sim y}c_{x,y}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{v_{j}}\lambda(t,x/N)\pi_{x,y}\left(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}(z_{j}-x_{j})\tau_{z}F_{N,i}\right). Here F_{N,i} is the i -th component of F_{N} and \Omega_{x,y} is defined as follows (7.28) \displaystyle\Omega_{x,y}(\eta) \displaystyle:=\nabla\lambda(t,x/N)\cdot\bigg((y-x)(\eta_{y}-\eta_{x})-\pi_{x,y}\Big(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\tau_{z}F_{N}\Big)\bigg). There exists a finite positive constant C(d,\lambda) depending on the dimension and the uniform ellipticity, such that the following estimate holds for the error term Q_{N}^{En}(\lambda,F_{N}) (7.29) \displaystyle|Q_{N}^{En}(\lambda,F_{N})|\leqslant \displaystyle CN^{-1}\Big(1+\|\nabla\lambda\|_{3,\infty}\Big)^{3}\Big(1+r(F_{N})^{d+2}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}\Big)^{3} \displaystyle+CN^{-1}\|\nabla\dot{\lambda}\|_{\infty}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}+CN^{-1}\|\dot{\lambda}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\lambda\|_{\infty}r(F_{N})^{d}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}, as long as the functions \lambda and F_{N} satisfy the bound (7.30) \displaystyle N^{-1}r(F_{N})^{d}\|\nabla\lambda\|_{\infty}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1. Remark 7.7 . The constant 1 on the right-hand side of ( 7.30 ) may be replaced by any other constant. The proof is postponed to Appendix C . At least, we read the decay of |Q_{N}^{En}(\lambda,F_{N})| in ( 7.29 ) when N\to\infty . We then analyze \Omega_{1},\Omega_{2} in the following paragraphs. 7.3.2. \Omega_{2} by the refined one-block estimate We now formulate the “refined” one-block estimate, that is, a fine error estimate for the replacement of the microscopic function \Omega_{2} of order O(1) by its ensemble average. We define the local sample average of \eta over the box x+\Lambda_{L} (7.31) \displaystyle\overline{\eta}_{x}^{L}:=\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{L}|}\sum_{y\in x+\Lambda_{L}}\eta_{y}. Here the x+\Lambda_{L} is regarded as its embedding in \mathbb{T}^{d}_{N} according to the context, and we take L as an odd integer for convenience. Then, for every local function \Omega\in\mathcal{F}_{0} , we define the error function in the replacement of \tau_{x}\Omega by its ensemble average at density \overline{\eta}_{x}^{L} as (7.32) \displaystyle\hat{\Omega}_{x}^{L}(\eta):=\tau_{x}\Omega(\eta)-\langle\Omega\rangle(\overline{\eta}_{x}^{L}), where we denote \langle\,\cdot\,\rangle(\rho) by \langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{\rho}=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\,\cdot\,] , \rho\in[0,1] . The error of the one-block estimate is mall thanks to the local ergodicity with respect to the integral of P^{f^{N}_{t}},t\in[0,T] Proposition 7.8 (Refined one-block estimate) . Let a_{t,x} be deterministic coefficients which satisfy (7.33) \forall t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d},\qquad|a_{t,x}|\leqslant M. We assume (7.34) L^{(d+2)/2}\leqslant\delta N for some \delta>0 small enough (the choice of \delta is determined later in Lemma C.1 ) and for all N large enough. Then, for every exponent \varepsilon\in(0,1) , there exists C=C(T,\varepsilon)>0 such that for every \Omega\in\mathcal{F}_{0} satisfying (recall the definition ( 7.2 )) (7.35) r(\Omega)\leqslant L^{\varepsilon}, we have the following estimate for all t\in[0,T] and N large enough (7.36) E\bigg[\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}N^{-d}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}a_{s,x}\hat{\Omega}^{L}_{x}(\eta^{N}(s))\,{\mathrm{d}}s\bigg|\bigg]\\ \leqslant CM(\|\Omega\|_{\infty}+1)\big(N^{-1}L^{(d+2)/2}+L^{-d}r(\Omega)^{d}\big). The proof of Proposition 7.8 is given in Appendix C as a combination of a known estimate, which is formulated in Lemma C.1 , and the equivalence of ensembles with precise convergence rate ( C.14 ). Proposition 7.8 can be applied to the specific term \Omega_{2} obtained in Lemma 7.6 to replace \int_{0}^{t}E^{f^{N}_{s}}[\Omega_{2}]\,{\mathrm{d}}s . The following corollary summarizes the error for this replacement. Roughly, ( 7.39 ) also exhibits a decay when N\to\infty . Corollary 7.9 . For t\in[0,T] , the integral \int_{0}^{t}E^{f^{N}_{s}}[\Omega_{2}]\,{\mathrm{d}}s can be replaced by (7.37) \displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}E^{f^{N}_{s}} \displaystyle\bigg[N^{-d}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{N}^{d}}\Big\{-\dot{\lambda}(s,x/N)\overline{\eta}_{x}^{L} \displaystyle\phantom{\sum_{x\in\Gamma_{N}}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla\lambda(s,x/N)\cdot\mathbf{c}(\overline{\eta}_{x}^{L};F_{N})\nabla\lambda(s,x/N)\right)\Big\}\bigg]\,{\mathrm{d}}s, where \mathbf{c}(\rho;F_{N}) is defined in ( 1.14 ). Moreover, given a constant \varepsilon\in(0,1) , and assume the following condition for the function F_{N}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{d} (with \mathbf{r}>0 as the range of the rate c_{x,y} in Hypothesis 1.1 ) (7.38) r(F_{N})+\mathbf{r}+1\leqslant L^{\varepsilon}, then there exists a positive finite constant C=C({T,\|\lambda\|_{\infty}},\varepsilon) such that the error for this replacement is dominated by (7.39) \displaystyle Q_{N,L}^{\Omega_{2},1}(\lambda,F_{N}):=C\big(N^{-1}L^{(d+2)/2}+L^{-d}(1+r(F_{N})^{d})\big) \displaystyle\qquad\times\Big(\|\dot{\lambda}\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla\lambda\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+r(F_{N})^{d}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty})^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}\lambda\|_{\infty}(1+r(F_{N})^{d+1}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty})\Big). Proof. We apply Proposition 7.8 to each term of \Omega_{2} . First, note that the ensemble averages of the third and fourth terms of \Omega_{2} vanish: \langle c_{x,y}(\eta_{x}-\eta_{y})\rangle_{\rho}=0,\quad\langle c_{x,y}\pi_{x,y}\Phi\rangle_{\rho}=0 for all \Phi=\Phi(\eta)\in\mathcal{F}_{0} . Then, ( 7.39 ) is obtained by gathering all errors given by ( 7.36 ) for each term of \Omega_{2} . Indeed, take M=\|\dot{\lambda}\|_{\infty} for the first term of \Omega_{2} , M(\|\Omega\|_{\infty}+1)\leqslant C\|\nabla\lambda\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+r(F_{N})^{d}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty})^{2} for the second term, M(\|\Omega\|_{\infty}+1)\leqslant C\|\nabla^{2}\lambda\|_{\infty} for the third term, and M(\|\Omega\|_{\infty}+1)\leqslant C\|\nabla^{2}\lambda\|_{\infty}(1+r(F_{N})^{d+1}\|F_{N}\|_{\infty}) for the fourth term. The condition ( 7.38 ) ensures ( 7.35 ) for each choice of \Omega .
